<meta>This page continues the heated debate between the English physician Robert Fludd (Robertus de Fluctibus) and the German astronomer Johannes Kepler. In the previous section, Fludd argued that physical harmony grows from small to large, much like biological life. Here, he responds to Kepler’s criticisms of his mystical "World Music," defending his Hermetic philosophy against Kepler’s mathematical-astronomical model.</meta>

TO THE KEPLERIAN APPENDIX.

## *Text XVI.*

*In his first volume, he distributes the whole world into three regions; where, following that most celebrated axiom of Hermes <note>The "Hermetic axiom" refers to the famous phrase "As above, so below," suggesting that the patterns of the heavens are reflected in the patterns of the earthly world.</note>, he makes the higher things similar or analogous to the lower. But in order for this analogy to succeed in everything, the things compared on both sides must often be dragged by the hair <note>A Latin idiom, "crinibus trahenda," meaning something is forced or far-fetched.</note>.*

### ANALYSIS.

<margin>Preface to Book 1.</margin>
Nay, indeed, we can hurl this very thing back at the Author [Kepler], who, in order to twist the five regular solids <term>the Platonic solids: the tetrahedron, cube, octahedron, dodecahedron, and icosahedron</term> to his own purpose, drags the intention of Aristotle and Pythagoras himself as if by the hair. He gathers infinite definitions, axioms, and propositions so that, by force of arms and struggling with all his might <note>original: "pedibus manibusque nixus," literally "striving with feet and hands"</note>, he may accommodate their symbols to his own inventions, even against the intentions of those very Philosophers. 

Consider also with what violence Nature is urged by him, while his Earth is suddenly raised to the stars, and thus a heavy thing is forced to tend upward. Likewise, he demands that from an opaque and immobile body, the Earth becomes a bright star and is understood to move between the spheres of Mars and Mercury. Indeed, he even claims it has its own soul <note>Kepler proposed the "Anima Terrae" (Soul of the Earth) to explain its geological and atmospheric activity</note> by which it commands the air and waters, and stirs up storms and the ether. Similarly, he demands that the Sun be rendered fixed and immobile, yet nevertheless be the source of motion and the center of the world, so that the Earth moves around it. As for what pertains to my own matters, the outcome will prove the deeds.

## *Text XVI.*

*Now, to come closer to the foundations upon which Robert Fludd builds his Worldly Music: first, he occupies the whole world and all three of its parts—the Empyrean <term>the highest, fiery heaven</term>, the Celestial, and the Elemental. I occupy only the celestial, and not even all of it, but only the motions of the Planets as if under the Zodiac.*

### ANALYSIS.

Yet he makes his own celestial part, which he chooses for his Harmony, as wide as my celestial and elemental parts combined, since he counts the Earth among the stars of heaven. From this it follows that his elemental part of the world is nothing but a portion of the vast heavens. 

Regarding the motion of the Planets, to which he commends the power of his entire Harmony: he does indeed acknowledge his Harmony in the heavens, but truly, he does not wish to look at or attend to the descent of that Harmony to us. Since he has dealt with the motions of the Planets, why does he admit in Text 5 that he has said almost nothing about the length and shortness of sounds, while he has seen in the meantime that the times of the Planets' motions are different? I, however, have established my discourses on ascending and descending harmony—that is, on the "exaltation" of things through first divine harmony, and then worldly harmony. For I know that I have heaven on earth; but he himself transfers and snatches his earth into heaven. In this way, the Poets imagined that Hercules, Astraeus, the Pleiades, the Argo, and indeed the Golden Fleece, Orion, Hydra, Aquilo <note>the North Wind</note>, and other such things were snatched up into the sky.

## *Text XVII.*

*He, relying on the ancients who believed the power of harmonies to reside in abstract numbers, is satisfied if he can demonstrate a concordance between parts, however he might encompass them in numbers, with no care as to what kind of units are accumulated in that number. I nowhere teach to seek harmonies where the things between which the harmonies exist cannot be measured by the same measure of quantity; so that the same proportion of quantity exists between them as exists between two strings of the same tension due to their length.*

### ANALYSIS.

<margin>Book 3, folio 4.</margin>
In this place, indeed, I see that the Author is completely ignorant of the true numbers of natural Harmony. Unless this were so, I know he would never have broken out into such words. For the triangle of Pythagoras <note>the Tetractys, a triangular figure of ten points</note> described by him is not made of plainly mercantile figures, but purely formal ones...

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s defense of his "Pythagorean" approach to numbers against Johannes Kepler’s more purely mathematical astronomy. Fludd argues that numbers are not just tools for counting, but are the spiritual blueprints of the universe.</meta>

<meta>catchword: titatis</meta>

...will find it depicted (if he wishes to consider the matter rightly) as far removed from abstractions. He himself tries to avoid abstract numbers in his harmony; and yet, it seems he treats nothing in numbers purely except abstractions, since mathematical numbers derived from lines, surfaces, and bodies—or roots, squares, and cubes—are no less abstract than those found in common algorithmic arithmetic. The wiser philosophers, namely Themistius, Boethius, Averroes, Pythagoras, and Plato, extol numbers so highly that they believe no one can philosophize correctly without them. But what that most noble number of which they spoke truly signifies, and its dispersal into members, is unknown to the common arithmetician who is only skilled in the merchant’s art of counting, or who is merely versed in common Music or Geometry and looks at things only on the surface. Instead, it is known by him who seeks out **formal proportions** <note>In Fludd's philosophy, "formal" refers to the "Form" or spiritual essence of a thing, rather than its "material" or physical substance.</note>, namely the degrees of the progression of form in nature from imperfection to the highest perfection. Therefore, those philosophers are not to be heard or understood as speaking of common or material and sensible numbers, but of the **formal number**, truly fiery and most familiar to reason. For regarding other numbers, the Pythagoreans, the Academics, and even Augustine himself cared nothing. This species of number they called Natural, formal, and rational, through which great miracles were performed in both divine and natural realms. Time itself, and all motion and action adapted to time, consists of such numbers; to these numbers are also referred all things subject to time and motion. Through these truly golden and formal numbers, harmonies and voices in Music have their proportion and consistency. Finally, all species of natural things, and those which are above nature, are bound together by certain formal numbers.

Those most learned in science best understood the mystery of these hidden numbers. They attributed the **Monad** <term>the number one, representing unity or God</term> or the unity of craftsmanship to God; the **Dyad** <term>the number two, representing duality or division</term> or duality to watery Matter; and afterwards the act of the **Triad** <term>the number three</term> to Form, or to light and the universal soul, which they called the "virgin." These, moreover, are those fiery and formal numbers which first flowed from the properties of the Most Holy Trinity into the fabric of the world: for watery Matter is joined to the luminous form by the "glue" of the divine Spirit and is called Heaven. Then, for man and his life, they attributed the **Hexad** <term>the number six</term> and the **Heptad** <term>the number seven</term>; and finally the **Ennad** <term>the number nine</term> to all creatures. From this, the number ten <note>original: "numerus denarius"</note>, including not only all the consonances of the world but even its dissonances, is attributed to the ten **Sephiroth** <term>In Jewish Kabbalah, the ten attributes or emanations through which the Infinite reveals itself</term> of the Sages. Over these they said ten names of God preside, expressing His ten properties in the world—consonant in mercy as much as dissonant in severity. For such is the progression of formal numbers, beyond which there never was, is, or shall be any other: 1, 2, 3, 4. Where 1 and 2 added together make 3; to which if 4 is added, they become 7. And this is 그 miraculous number in which the creation of the world was completed; for in it, the material or elementary square is united to the formal root, and the appetite of worldly matter is so fulfilled that it rests, being filled with the highest good. Hence, or in this respect, this number is given to the **Sabbath** or to rest. 

It is also sometimes a number of punishment, when the property of *Elohim* <note>A Hebrew name for God, often associated in mystical traditions with justice or judgment.</note> considered in the triple number is turned into wrath; and thus it is dissonant: *Whoever kills Cain shall be avenged sevenfold (Genesis 4).* Similarly, other mysteries are contained under it: *For a just man falls seven times (Proverbs 24).* *After seven more days I will send rain upon the earth (Genesis 7:4).* *The ark rested in the seventh month (Genesis 8).* *Noah waited seven days after the return of the dove.* *Wisdom built her house with seven pillars (Proverbs 9:1).* *Moses sprinkled the altar seven times with the oil of unction (Leviticus 8:11).* *Elisha commanded Naaman to wash seven times to be cured of leprosy (2 Kings 5:10).* *Miriam, affected by leprosy, was shut out for seven days (Leviticus 14).* <note>The story of Miriam's leprosy is found in Numbers 12, though Fludd cites Leviticus 14, which deals with the laws of cleansing lepers.</note> And the Holy Scriptures have recounted infinite other mysteries. However, this naming of the number seven is understood not in the common way, but in the mystical and formal sense. For, as the letter kills but the spirit gives life, so also is the difference between the common and the spiritual number.

But let us proceed with our purpose. 1, 2, 3 make 6; to which if 4 is added (which is unity), it becomes 7; or by the addition of the quaternary number in its full value to 6, 10 is produced. Beyond which, as Aristotle testifies, there is no further progression: for all numbers that follow are mere compositions of these. In these formal numbers, therefore, all things are included. And consequently, whoever knows how to join vocal and natural numbers with divine ones, and to temper them into the same consonance, this person will be able to work wonders through such a number, and also to know the same, as those most skilled in **Natural Magic** have confidently written. For these are the true numbers in which lies the secret of the essence of all Harmony, without the knowledge of which it is impossible to judge rightly of Music, whether worldly <term>Musica Mundana, the "music of the spheres"</term> or artificial. And certainly, although I acknowledge myself hardly worthy of the knowledge of so great a mystery, nevertheless I have labored mightily to adapt the proportion of such numbers in my works. Of which, indeed, the curious reader will perceive that I have dealt more copiously in my *Microcosmic treatise in the booklet on divine numbers*. But I would not want you to believe that I have turned the thread of my discourse there to common numbers, but rather to the proportions of the mystery of Nature. 

We say therefore to the Author [Kepler] that he perceives our intention poorly by guessing that I received the power of my Harmonies from abstract numbers. But indeed I see that he is so uplifted in his own opinion that he either holds the more relaxed matters treated by us in contempt, or at least is least able to grasp them, or to look into their inner shrines with his "mathematical eyes." I say, therefore, that I have had greater care in the accumulation of **formal unities** for explaining and unshelling the truth of worldly Harmony than he could possibly have, who is versed only in the letter of the matter and seems to taste nothing of the formal spirit. For how is it possible for him to judge worldly Music, who is utterly ignorant of its essence? But let us return to his "excellence" in Music, which he seems to trumpet so loudly to the world. He himself does not admit harmonies where the things between which there are harmonies cannot be measured by the same measure of quantity...

...of quantity, the same proportion exists between them as between two strings of the same tension on account of their length. Indeed, he produced a proportion of this kind in *Chapter 4, Book 3.* Among strings stretched equally, those sounds are deeper which are longer, and those sharper which are shorter. In this, he seems to admit no proportional Music except where there is a difference in length between strings of the same measure or width. However, common experience teaches that this is false; since two strings of equal tension and the same length, but of different or diverse thickness or size, are accustomed to return various harmonies in the deep and the sharp; concerning which I spoke more broadly in the *Analysis of Text 13.* Therefore, here the difference in length is not the cause, but the difference in size. Whence I also assigned the bass and deeper sounds to the denser and thicker matter of the world, and the sharper sounds to the thinner spirit of the world: for the sake of demonstrating these things, we have not ineptly drawn the figure of a pyramid. <note>Fludd refers to his famous "Interpenetrating Pyramids" diagram, where one pyramid represents the descent of Divine Light/Form and the other represents the ascent of Matter/Darkness.</note>

## Text XVIII.

*For that reason, he divided the whole world into three equal parts by the measure of the radius, knowing well enough that those parts are by no means equal; but he did so only because the first unit is the Elemental world, the second the ethereal world, and the third the Empyrean: and truly, units cannot be depicted otherwise than by the equality of lines. But as for me, unless astronomy testifies to that same quantitative measure of units, I by no means use them as units for numbering harmonic proportions.* <note>Kepler argues that Fludd's three-fold division of the universe (Earth, Stars, Heaven) is a symbolic choice rather than one based on the actual astronomical distances measured by a radius.</note>

### ANALYSIS.

Here Johannes Kepler seems to rebuke me sharply, because I distributed the diameter of the world-machine into three equal parts for the discovery of my Macrocosmic Harmony. For, he says, the first unit makes the Elemental world, the second the Ethereal world, and the third the Empyrean; he concludes this is erroneous because units cannot be depicted otherwise than by equality of lines. It is very easy to respond to this objection of his. For, if the ethereal heaven is held to be the male, and the elemental region the female—whose womb is the earth—it is necessary that there be the same and most exact proportion between the ethereal heaven and that which is below. This is because the passion <term>passion: here used in the Aristotelian sense of the "capacity to be acted upon" or to receive form</term> in the female must correspond in the most exact degree to the action in the male, according to that infallible axiom of Plato: <margin>matter is given in form</margin> *Form is given according to the merit of the matter.* <note>original: "Secundum meritum materiae datur forma"</note> Truly, there is no generation except among things compatible in nature. But indeed, heaven acts upon the lower things and gives them form; the lower things, however, suffer equally and receive the forms of heaven by which they may be informed. 

But, to come closer to the matter, he denies that my units are equal because they cannot be depicted or measured by the equality of lines. Here indeed the Author has betrayed his ignorance in the true knowledge of natural units, because he seems not to know the weights of nature, without which one can reach neither the secret of Harmony nor the mysteries of celestial motions. <margin>Hermes</margin> For Hermes says (and most rightly indeed): *That which is above is like that which is below, and vice versa;* <note>The "Emerald Tablet" of Hermes Trismegistus, a foundational text for alchemists and Hermetic philosophers.</note> that is, a thick unit is the same as a subtle unit, and a subtle unit is the same as a thick one. That is to say: a thin spiritual unit is the same as a moderately thick one, and this is the same as the terrestrial unit, the densest of all. 

These units, nevertheless, are not measured by a common and equal mathematical line, unless such a measure is used for the sake of demonstration. For example: if we had two handfuls of earth, one of which was to be kept in its thick and terrestrial nature, and the other reduced by thinning into water, surely that one handful of earth left in its nature will be equal to that other single one reduced into water, by reason of its weight—in which the proportions of the wise truly consist. And although that single handful is reduced by thinning into ten handfuls of water, and consequently requires a larger place than it had before; nevertheless, its proportion in weight is the same, insofar as it can be reduced by art into that same earth as it was before. Similarly, that water reduced into air will require ten times as much place and space for its receptacle as it had before; and yet in formal proportion, it does not differ from one thick handful; and so it is with the rest. 

Whence it must be concluded, against that objection of the Author, that units of the same bulk and element require equality of mathematical lines in their dimension; but indeed, those same units differing in bulk, and with their substances changed in thickness and thinness—so that, for instance, one of them remains thick while the other is thinned—are not measured by the identity of mathematical lines, but by weights. And this is partly what Hermes understands in his enigmatic speech, when he says, *the higher relates to itself as the lower and vice versa:* that is, one subtle thing is the same as one thick thing, or heaven is the same as earth, and the spiritual is the same as the corporeal, and the thin heaven is the same as the thick heaven. That is, they are to be numbered by formal measure—which is by the proportions of **mystic weights**—and not by the identity of common or numerical, linear, superficial, or corporeal measure, which measures are known to wander far from the intentions of the Wise. Therefore, the most exact measures of these things, from which their Harmonies, the mysteries of nature, and the motions of the world are elicit...

Fludd argues that his measurements are "essential" and "alchemical" rather than merely spatial.</meta>

# ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE

...are those which are called the **Weights of the Sages** <note>original: *pondera Sapientum*; these refer to the specific alchemical proportions used to transmute elements</note>; to whom alone, and to no others, it is given to make changes in nature from one element into another while identity remains. Indeed, by these weights, from one handful of earth and nine <note>represented by the symbol for Mercury (☿) in the original, often used as a shorthand for the volatile principle or a specific measure here</note> of air, ten handfuls of water are made; and from one handful of water and nine of fire, ten handfuls of air are made, ascending through the **scale of vivification** from gross nature into that which is simple and high. For in this way, nature was accustomed to produce the heavens themselves from gross and terrestrial matter through the exaltation of the identity of the elements into the nature of the heavens. Then, from one handful of fire and nine of water, ten of "dead air" are made, which is "living water" <note>Fludd is using alchemical paradoxes to describe the condensation and rarefaction of spiritual matter</note>; and from nine of the element of earth, ten of "glorified water" are made.

But these proportions of weights must be understood with wisdom; for this is that sole doctrine without which no measure, proportion, or temperament can be acquired. For this proportion is that **golden chain** <note>The *Aurea Catena*, a traditional concept of the hierarchical connection between God and the physical world</note> and the wheel of the circles of the whole age, through which Nature herself is governed in the rotation and revolution of all her instruments. Without this gift of wisdom, therefore, no one skilled in the science of Astronomy can rightly judge the position and movements of the stars; no one can know the constitution of the world, or the force of the elements, or the beginning, middle, and end of times, the mutations of the solstices and the varieties of seasons, the circuit of the year, the natures of living things, the rages of wild beasts, the forces of winds and spirits, the reason of storms, the very thoughts of men, the differences of plants, and the faculties of roots, or whatever things are hidden or manifest; according to that passage in *Wisdom 7:21.* 

These are those distinguished gifts of wisdom, without which no one can rightly penetrate into any science from those **seven liberal foundations** of universal knowledge; instead, men led by opinion fall into infinite errors. And hence it is said, *that wisdom has built her house by hewing out her seven pillars (Proverbs 9:1)* and *that she is to be preferred by the learned over money and the most choice dug-up gold.* Whence the wise man says: *Wisdom is better than rubies, and all things that may be desired are not to be compared to it (Proverbs 8:11).* 

Truly, this wisdom is more beautiful than **Kepler’s sun**, which he made the source of motion, and it is found to be prior to every arrangement of the stars. Nor is it a wonder, since it is a *pure emanation of the glory of the Almighty God (Wisdom 7).* Toward the inquiry of this wisdom, therefore, my "units" tend, so that I may reach the highest summit of that unity. For so the sages say: *all things are one and the same in God and through God; who is the sole truth, from whom all things proceed into multitude through multiplication and dispersion, and into whom likewise all things return through exaltation and resolution into simplicity.*

Therefore, although my philosophy may seem common, yet under its shadow better things will appear through right inquiry. At any rate, I respond to the Author's <note>Kepler's</note> objections thus: that my "units" are not to be measured by the same or equal linear dimensions. Rather, I used them so that, in the manner of a **pyramid**, I might more exactly describe the formula for the refinement of matter. But if I were to explain the dimension of each sphere of the elements and heavens according to its linear proportions (if indeed that were possible), no paper would have been able to contain my figures. 

We say, therefore, that if the elemental heaven were refined into an ethereal nature, then that "one" would fill the immense space of the ethereal heaven. Yet it does not follow that because it does not occupy so ample a space, it is therefore not "one" in natural proportion (which consists in **weight**), even though it differs in linear dimension. And we, taught by experience, know that earth can be reduced into heaven, and yet the nature of the proportion of its substance remains the same as it was before; just as a poor man exalted to the status of a Bishop remains the same person. Similarly, a small amount of water reduced into a large vapor through distillation returns again to its former quantitative nature through condensation; namely, so that although it occupies the greatest space in smoke, but a tiny one in water, its "unity" in the smoke does not differ from its "unity" in the water, except in space alone.

But why should I use more words in this cause? Surely all of this—indeed, even the response to this objection of his—is best explained by me in *Chapter 16, Book 2, Treatise 1, on the Fabric of the Macrocosm, Experiment 3*, where we have described the ratio of our units and the difference in their subtlety and thickness with precision. If the Author had read that chapter, perhaps he would not have judged my units so rashly. From these things, therefore, we conclude that the Author betrays his own ignorance in this context, rather than being able to justly impute any fault to me. For as to what he seems to assert—that units cannot be painted or described without equality of lines—it is clear from this that he investigates things more **mathematically and quantitatively** than truly **physically**, or according to the essence of natural things; that is, according to the measure of light in spiritual matter, or of sulfur in its Mercury. Let his "imaginary astronomy" testify to that same quantitative measure of units; I, indeed, content with my physical way, will adhere to my natural measures, equipped with essential weights.

### Text XIX.

> *He, however, insisting on his principles and leading out a pyramid from its base (the great circle of the Earth), established its vertex at the very apex of heaven; and its height being divided into three equal parts (just as if he had units that were truly equal), he counts how many parts come to the Empyrean, how many to the Celestial, and how many to the Elemental. For the top of the Elemental region is, in this way, twice as far from the top of the Celestial as it is from the top of the Empyrean: And in divi-*

<summary>Fludd defends his alchemical use of "units" against Kepler's mathematical criticisms, arguing that his cosmic pyramid represents the essential quality and "weight" of matter rather than literal physical distance. He asserts that spiritual wisdom and alchemical proportions are superior to the purely quantitative measurements of astronomers.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Fludd’s direct rebuttal to Johannes Kepler. In the previous page, Fludd introduced the "Weights of the Sages" and the alchemical transformation of elements. Here, he addresses Kepler’s specific mathematical critique of his "pyramidal" diagrams, which Fludd uses to map the relationship between God (Form/Light) and the physical world (Matter/Darkness).</meta>

*...by the division of the pyramid, with respect to the axis, three equal parts come from the three regions; but with respect to the triangle through the axis, one unit goes to the Empyrean, three to the Celestial, and five units to the [Elementary] <note>The original Latin repeats "Empyreo" here, likely an error for "Elementari," the lowest realm, following the 1-3-5 mathematical progression</note>. Finally, with respect to the space or the bulk of the figure, one unit goes to the Empyrean, seven to the Celestial, and nineteen units to the Elementary. What shall I say now of the other, contrary Pyramid of Light, whose base is the adorable Trinity itself at the highest summit of the Empyrean heaven, but whose vertex is placed upon the earth itself?*

---

### ANALYSIS.

In this context, like a most inconsistent **Proteus** <note>A shape-shifting sea god from Greek mythology; Fludd is accusing Kepler of constantly changing his arguments to avoid the truth</note>, he seems to vary in his words. At one moment he treats the equality of the division of the pyramid's parts, then he inquires after its length, and finally he examines the three-fold dimension with his mathematical industry; in all of these things, he wanders far from my true intention. Concerning the rationale of our "units," we shall speak abundantly to the wise in the analysis of the following text, where much will be said on this subject in few words. Then he says that I have counted how many parts come to the **Empyrean** <note>The highest, most spiritual heaven, often associated with pure fire or light</note>, how many to the **Celestial** <note>The middle realm of the planets and stars</note>, and how many to the **Elementary** <note>The lowest realm, consisting of the four physical elements: earth, water, air, and fire</note>. In this, too, he is deceived regarding my intention. For my demonstration is not intended to treat the physical magnitude of the parts of the heavens, but rather the proportion of the **attenuation of spiritualized matter** <note>Fludd means the process by which physical matter becomes thinner, purer, and more like spirit as it moves upward toward God</note>. I am saying that the more the matter of the world tends upward, the more subtle, pure in its nature, and spiritual it is rendered. 

Therefore, my pyramidal proportions were not intended to show that there is "less" matter in one heaven than in another (for I have affirmed that the units in the elementary heaven are the same in weight as those in the celestial and the Empyrean). Rather, through the arrangement of the portions of the pyramid, I intended to indicate the proportion of the refinement and purity of matter as it ascends—for example, the degrees of proportion in the movement from imperfection toward perfection. He [Kepler] moves on to an examination of the pyramid's axis; this examination, although it pertains to a mathematical pyramid, is of little relevance here—no more than an inquiry into a solid pyramid using numbers. For who among the beginners or novices in Geometry is ignorant of the numerical proportion of the parts of a solid pyramid to one another? 

But I brought forth a Geometric figure for the sake of demonstration, so that I might more easily show the world the progress of **physical nature** within mundane matter. I certainly did not intend for the reader to immediately jump from my physical intention to a mathematical counting of the pyramid. Nor did I introduce the Geometric pyramid in my demonstrations with the mind that you should consider the identity of its matter, or compare the parts of its solidity to one another, or measure its length; but rather that you should observe its figure and its proportion in descending and ascending—in moving from imperfection to the goal of perfection. 

For in this way, we understand the proportions of perfection through the degrees of heaven, and the scale of imperfection through the stations of the elements distant from one another, before the limits of perfection are reached. And since, to the naked eye, a mathematical pyramid and all its parts are made of the same material—that is, one part is homogenous with another—I divided it into equal parts for a better explanation of the subject. However, that matters little to my purpose, which was to explain the physical motion of physical matter from the **gross to the subtle**. Therefore, we have expressed the nature of my intention regarding the true physical pyramid as it exists in the nature of things in *Chapter 17, Book 5 of the Physics of the History of the Macrocosm.* 

And since I perceived that the difficulty of my intention might perhaps have brought a poor opinion of my labors to the world (due to a less than clear explanation of the truth, or of my mind and meaning), I therefore dedicated an entire book in the final volume of my *Technical History of the Macrocosm* to the explanation of physical pyramids, both formal and material. There, I fully clarified and set forth my intention regarding the natural pyramid. We say, therefore, that our pyramidal contemplation is more "formal" <note>In the Aristotelian sense, "formal" refers to the "form" or soul of a thing, rather than its physical "matter"</note> than can be grasped under mathematical consideration. It denotes the hidden progression of form within matter toward its purification, refinement, and its guidance toward maturity and perfection from imperfection, and from the coarse and dense toward the thin. 

And in this indeed lies the entire intention of the Sages regarding the proportions and measures of **mystical harmony**; nor does it differ from the progression of nature in this world. I make the base of that formal pyramid the **Seat of God**, because there the perfection of all matter is consummated, and matter itself is reduced from mundane multiplicity back to divine unity. Such will be the consummation (or rather, the transfiguration) of the world, and such was its beginning. 

One can see, therefore, that just as a material pyramid is more powerful in its form the more it tends upward (as may be gathered from my figures), so also the world itself and everything in the world abounds more in **form** the more they are moved toward purity and subtlety from impurity and coarseness. And thus they always ascend—not indeed by mathematical measures from the bottom upward (that is, from place to place), but from imperfection to perfection, from impurity to purity, from a humble degree to an exalted state, from rawness to complete maturity, from darkness to light, from earth to heaven, from evil to good, and finally, from the **Devil to God**. In this way, a Bishop is exalted without changing his location; and thus indeed...

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s point-by-point rebuttal of Johannes Kepler. On the previous page, Fludd was describing the mathematical proportions of his "Pyramid of Light" and "Pyramid of Matter." Here, he concludes a sentence about the world's progression toward divine perfection before moving into "Text 20," which is a direct quote from Kepler’s critique of Fludd's work. Fludd then provides an "Analysis" to defend his Hermetic philosophy against Kepler’s purely mathematical astronomy.</meta>

...the world and all things in it are situated, before they are promoted to more-than-perfection, in which God rules and dwells as the more-than-perfect.

### TEXT XX.

> *When he <note>Kepler is referring to Fludd here</note> mixes these two pyramids together and draws musical proportions from that mixture, he attempts something very different from the purpose of my work. For he compares light (the giver of form and soul) and matter—two things most different from each other, in which quantities are by no means present in the same respect. For my part, I do not admit any terms for forming harmonic proportions in the world unless they receive quantities in the same respect—for example, the daily motions of Mars and Jupiter.*

### ANALYSIS.

Regarding my consonances <note>In this context, "consonances" refers to the musical and mathematical harmonies Fludd believes exist between the spiritual and material worlds</note>, he considers them false and entirely different from his own purpose because they arise from the mixing of light with matter, which he claims are "most different." Consequently, he infers that the quantities of worldly matter and form are by no means present in the same respect. 

This assertion of his indicates an even greater ignorance of the deep science of natural things than those which preceded it. For first, this is certain and approved by all Philosophers: that the "appetite" of matter is to be informed <note>In Aristotelian philosophy, "informed" means to be given a specific shape, nature, or soul by a "Form"</note> by light, just as it is the nature of light (or the lucid form) to inform matter itself. Therefore, when the matter of the world was completed, it was undoubtedly done through this lucid informing. From this it follows that "matter in act"—which is called "secondary matter" by **Aristotle** and "Water" by **Moses** <note>Fludd is equating the "primeval waters" of Genesis 1:2 with the philosophical concept of matter</note>—must retain a sufficient portion of form in order to exist, to live, and to remain as it is. 

Furthermore, it has been said according to the opinion of **Plato** that forms are given according to the "merit" of the matter; indeed, there is no generation except from things that are compatible in the nature of both form and matter. From this it is clear that lowly matter possesses a lowly form, while exalted matter possesses an exalted form. We also say that matter naturally "suffers" (receives action) just as form "acts." According to the **Wise One** <note>likely referring to Solomon or the author of the Book of Wisdom</note>, the same conjunction occurs between matter and form as occurs between a female and male animal. 

Furthermore, we say that the matter of the element of fire is more worthy than that of air because it retains a larger portion of form; its material substance was more disposed to receive a multitude of form than that of air. In the same proportion, the matter of air is more worthy than that of water, and water more than earth; similarly, the heavens are more worthy than the substance of fire. From these points, it is evident that matter in all things adapts itself to its form according to its dignity, just as the "dominion" of the form relates to the matter. For there is no diversity in matter except because of a greater or lesser proportion of form within it. Indeed, matter receives its dignity from form, just as form receives its dominion from the matter it rules.

From this it is clearly evident that a most exact natural proportion exists between form and matter. However, this agreement does not admit the same "quantities" insofar as Form is not measured by the common measure of Geometry, but by an **Ideal** measure. Because form is invisible (even though it acts visibly), it cannot be depicted with geometric lines except through an analogical representation. 

And yet, its measurement is most certain. For, just as the dimension and pyramidal figure of physical matter is shaped, such also is the formal figure—namely, pyramidal, and of the same dimension. For the effect of a formal action is rendered sensible through the alterations of its visible matter; because that which makes a thing "such," is itself "more such." Since Form gave matter such a figure because of its own proportional presence within it, it follows that the Form itself, existing ideally within the material pyramid, is equal to it in both figure and dimension. 

In this way, we say the soul is in the whole and in every part of the animal body. Thus, the soul shaped the dimension of the animal body according to its own hidden proportion. This is also how the soul (or Form) exists within the material pyramid, which can admit a geometric or quantitative measure. Does the Author <note>Kepler</note> himself not seem to acknowledge this in his 3rd book, page 4, in his description of a "numerical pyramid"? There, he seems to assign one side of the triangle—namely, the side of odd numbers—to the soul or form, and the other side (equal to it and composed of even numbers) to matter. Thus, he makes the side of matter in the pyramidal triangle of equal quantity with the formal side. 

**Pythagoras** and **Plato** also handed down the **Monad** <note>The number One, representing unity or God</note> to God the Creator, the **Dyad** <note>The number Two, representing duality or division</note> to matter, and the **Triad** <note>The number Three</note> to form. Indeed, the ancient Sages knew that the whole mystery of worldly Harmony lay hidden in these proportional numbers, which are purely formal. From these things it is also quite clear that "watery matter" and "lucid form" are not "most different," as he made them out to be, but that they agree perfectly in action and passion—no differently than a man and a woman, from whose union natural generation occurs. And this is indeed the power of **Worldly Harmony**: namely, to produce harmonic consonances or proportions of actions and passions between matter and form in every region of the world.

<summary>Fludd defends his use of "pyramids" to explain the universe, arguing against Kepler that spirit (Form) and physical matter are not "diverse" opposites but are harmoniously joined like male and female. He cites Aristotle, Moses, and Kepler’s own previous writings to argue that matter is shaped by the "Ideal" proportions of light.</summary>
<keywords>Harmony of the World, Form and Matter, Pyramidal proportions, Robert Fludd, Johannes Kepler, Monad, Dyad, Triad, Aristotelian physics, Hermeticism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s defense of his "Pyramid of Light" and "Pyramid of Matter" against the criticisms of the astronomer Johannes Kepler. Fludd has just argued that matter naturally desires to be shaped by light and form. On this page, he addresses Kepler’s claim that planetary motions are the only valid basis for harmony and defends the mystical significance of the number four, which Kepler had mocked.</meta>

->AD APPEND. KEPPLERIAN.<-
<note>original: "To the Keplerian Appendix"</note>

From here, he <note>Kepler</note> proceeds to the arrangement of his own harmonies, which he says he will not admit unless they receive quantities in the same respect; and in this way, he chooses the material proportions of the world, but seems to reject the forms, because they cannot be measured by a common quantity. And yet, he continues to a formal example—namely, the daily motions of Mars and Jupiter. I ask you to notice how poorly these things agree with what he said before. For he says that my Harmony is very different from his own because I derive my foundations from light and matter; and yet he himself seems to derive his Harmony from motion, which is nothing other than the effect of light. For from light arises the act, and from the act, motion proceeds. Thus it is clear that the cause of his Harmony is merely the effect of my cause, just as heat is said to be the effect of fire. Indeed, the light in the body of a planet is that which moves it—that is, by a greater or lesser amount of light, or form, or the moving and acting soul in the body of the planet, which exists in a proportion suited to the arrangement of the mass of matter. Furthermore, regarding his claim that he does not admit any Harmonies except those that receive quantities in the same respect, we have already demonstrated above that this can be otherwise, using the example of two strings of the same length and tension, but of varying or different magnitude <note>Fludd means thickness or mass of the strings</note>. We conclude, therefore, that his assertion in this context is inconsistent.

## Text XXI.

*There is no less diversity between us in this: that he <note>Fludd</note> assigns four degrees of obscurity and darkness to the Elemental region, because, he says, any part has four quarters—surely no less than three thirds and five fifths! And now, he claims that all four are in the Earth, three in water (making it already transparent), two in the air, and one in fire.*

### ANALYSIS.

In this place, the dignity of the number four <note>the quaternary</note> is called into question. Indeed, the persistence of the Author <note>Kepler</note> compels me to defend it with all my might, as far as the smallness of my talent allows. The Divinity has celebrated the excellence of this number above all others, which leads me to hold and recognize it as divine; but Nature herself, the handmaid of Divinity, has also declared its wonderful effects through the noble mathematical sciences—namely Arithmetic, Geometry, Music, and Astronomy. Therefore, if we diligently scrutinize the praises of this number in divinity, we will perceive in the first place that this square number is adapted to God the Father, in whom the mystery of the entire most holy Trinity is included. For the simple and first proportion of the quaternary—that is, of one to one—denotes the symbol of the <term>Monad</term> <note>the "One" or the indivisible source</note> of the supersubstantial paternal essence, from which the second Monad proceeding begat a Son equal to Himself; and this second procession is also simple, like 1 from 1. However, the proportion of two to two, which is the second procession from the simple ones, denotes the Holy Spirit proceeding from the two—namely, from the Father and the Son. The ineffable name <term>יהוה</term> <note>the Tetragrammaton, or the four-letter name of God: Yahweh</note> clearly expresses these processions in the number four: where the double *He* or <term>ה</term> denotes the procession from *Jod* <term>י</term> the Father and *Vau* <term>ו</term> the Son. Hence, this name alone, expressing the essence of God and no other, is called the *Tetragrammaton*. And this is why this number is called the *head and fountain of all Divinity* by the Sages. Nature herself, which draws her origin from divinity, also claims this number for her own principle. This is the very thing the Pythagoreans sang, who called this number the *perpetual fountain of Nature*, as appears from these following verses, by which the Pythagoreans were accustomed to swear:

> *I swear to you with a pure mind by the holy four,*
> *The fountain of Eternal Nature and the parent of the soul.*

But I will now say in a word how this is: the Pythagoreans did not make "duality" a number, but a confusion of unities. Therefore, they established its square as the first even number, and not without reason: for since one unity denotes the divine form or act, and the other denotes the divine power or matter, it is necessary that power appear out of the darkness through the act. Of these two unities, the first (through the bond of the triple Unity) made the universal substance of the world according to the property of the most holy Trinity. But truly, because the first square was from the number two, the progression of nature was into that number four, which is a double proportion to the number two, or the square of two; and in this way, the universal substance of the world, the water, was divided into 4 Elements distinct from one another. From this primary even number, the progress is made to the first Cube in the nature of things, which is the number eight, or the quaternary doubled, denoting the compositions made from the Elements—just as the elements themselves, like a square, proceeded from the number two, which denotes simple matter and simple form divided from each other. From here, therefore, the proces—

Fludd then provides a mystical defense of the number four (the quaternary), linking it to the Trinity, the Tetragrammaton (YHWH), and Pythagorean philosophy as the foundational structure of the physical and divine worlds.</summary>

--- Page 40 ---
<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s philosophical defense of his system of universal harmony against the astronomer Johannes Kepler. Having previously argued that form and light are the primary movers of the universe, Fludd now transitions into a detailed mathematical and mystical justification for the number four—the "quaternary"—as the foundational structure of all reality, from the elements of nature to the intervals of music and the symbols of alchemy.</meta>

# ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE
<meta>catchword: Furthermore</meta>

...there proceeded four natures, steps related to the four Elements, namely **Being, Living, Feeling, and Understanding**; the 4 Cardinal points of the world, the 4 Triplicities <note>Groups of three zodiac signs associated with one of the four elements (Fire, Earth, Air, Water).</note> in the heavens, the 4 primary qualities beneath the heavens, and the 4 seasons of the year. Indeed, all of Nature is encompassed by 4 terms: namely, **substance, quality, quantity, and motion**. Finally, a fourfold arrangement usually fills the entire universe: seminal unity, natural sprouting, maturing form, and the completed composite. 

By infinitely many other reasons, one may observe how this property of the Quaternary Nature looks back to the same cause. By these proofs, we can clearly demonstrate that the number four is more suitable for choosing the division and distinction of "moist nature" <note>A term often used by Fludd to refer to prime matter or the receptive, material part of the universe.</note> than either the number three or five. The eminence of this number above others is also proven by Arithmetic; for through that science, its double proportion is best explained. The first of these is one to two, and the second is two to four, just as its origin is produced and born from a twin procession and proportion: namely, one to one and two to two. It begins, therefore, from unity, and finishes in the quaternary. 

Indeed, all other numbers are contained within this number: for one and one make 2; and from 1 and 2 arises 3; and from three with unity, 4 is born. Thus stand 1, 2, 3, 4, in which all the mysteries of the whole world and of Nature itself, as well as the dimensions of Arithmetic, are contained. For from 3 and 4, the number seven <note>The <term>septenary</term></note> comes forth, which if considered formally is clearly mystical and full of secrets. From 2 and 3, the number five results by addition; from 1, 2, and 3, the number six; from 1, 3, and 4, the number eight; from 2, 3, and 4, the number nine; and finally, from the gathering of the whole natural progression 1, 2, 3, 4, the number ten <note>The <term>denary</term>, or the Pythagorean Tetractys, where 1+2+3+4=10.</note> is produced, beyond whose naming there is no further departure. 

From these, therefore, all Geometric progressions and Musical proportions arise, such as 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10; and 1, 3, 6, 9; and 1, 4, 8. Indeed, he who rightly understands the use of this natural progression 1, 2, 3, 4 in formal contemplation will not find hidden the mystery of the 7 days of creation, nor why the Sun was made on the fourth day, nor how 3 and 4 constitute a number—whether it be seven, ten, or four—among rational numbers. He will see why the number four is the day of the Sabbath or rest, and why it is the day of the sun; likewise, how in true natural operation, three denotes and constitutes the number six, and why the six days of creation are significant. He will search out the reason for critical days and climacteric years; <note>A "climacteric year" was thought to be a critical turning point in a person's life, usually occurring in multiples of 7 or 9.</note> indeed, by considering 4 in place of unity, he will see with eyes as if opened wide the procreation of the seven Planets in the world, and many other wonders. 

In Geometry, its power is infinite, insofar as it encompasses this part of Mathematics in 4 terms: namely, **point, line, surface, and body**. From this, we also see that original Geometric Cube proceed, from whose center the Author <note>God, as the Great Architect.</note> himself produced the rest, just like the 4 elements from the womb of Chaos. For from the multiplication of a square, the Cube is produced, which he acknowledged to be the first-born and to contain the logic of the whole. Since this is so, it was necessary that we choose the quaternary number in our divisions—since the Cube is resolved into it as if into its first elements (namely, squares)—rather than into 3 thirds or 5 fifths. This is because in corruption, the resolution of the composite (or Cube) occurs into the 4 elements (or the square), just as conversely, in generation, the natural progression is from the square to the Cube. 

Finally, the power of this number is found most exactly in the science of Music, insofar as it contains within itself all the harmony of Music. For in the "double" ratio, such as 1 to 2, consists the <term>Diapason</term> <note>The interval of an octave.</note>; in the "one-and-a-half" ratio, as 2 to 3, consists the <term>Diapente</term> <note>The interval of a perfect fifth.</note>; and in the "one-and-a-third" ratio, as 3 to 4, resides the <term>Diatessaron</term> <note>The interval of a perfect fourth.</note>. 

Furthermore, from the quaternary number and its root, all the proportions of compound consonances arise: as the Diapason with a Diapente <note>A compound interval of a twelfth.</note> relates in a triple ratio as 2, 4, 6. For between 2 and 6, the triple proportion is composed of the double (2 and 4) and the one-and-a-half (4 and 6). But the double Diapason <note>An interval of two octaves.</note> is found in the quadruple ratio, as 2, 4, 8. Moreover, the Diatessaron and the Diapente together constitute one Diapason, as 2, 3, 4. From these things, one may see that all proportions in Music receive their virtues from the quaternary number and its root, and either fall into or rise out of its dimensions. 

Finally, if we look to mystical Astronomy, we shall perceive the entire power of the quaternary number in it, and most clearly so; since its whole secret is contained in the **Hieroglyphic Monad**, which bears the symbols of the Moon, the Sun, the elements, and fire, as if those four things which act and are acted upon in the world to bring about the constant changes by which both corruption and generation occur within it. The figure is of this sort:

<image-desc>The "Monas Hieroglyphica" symbol, a complex glyph consisting of a crescent moon (top), a circle with a central point (sun), a cross (the four elements), and two intersecting semi-circles at the base (fire). Labels are provided to the right of each component part.</image-desc>

In this symbolism, we see first the sign of the quaternary number in the cross, through the arrangement of four lines at a common point. When joined with the number three (denoting the Moon, Sun, and Fire), it produces the number seven; this can also be provided by the 4 elements. And yet, this number seven is in itself nothing other than the quaternary number considered formally.

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s philosophical defense of his system of universal harmony against the astronomer Johannes Kepler. Having previously argued that form and light are the primary movers of the universe, Fludd now transitions into a detailed mathematical and mystical justification for the number four—the "quaternary"—as the foundational structure of all reality, from the elements of nature to the intervals of music and the symbols of alchemy.</meta>

->TO THE KEPLERIAN APPENDIX<-

Furthermore, those more skilled in common Astronomy have observed a matter of great importance when they divided the Zodiac into four triplicities <note>Groups of three zodiac signs associated with one of the four elements: Fire, Earth, Air, and Water.</note>. We conclude, therefore, that the Sages called this number the <term>Tetractys</term> <note>original: "Tetractin"; a Pythagorean symbol of four rows of dots that add up to ten, representing the organization of the universe.</note>; and they preferred it in power above all other numbers, seeing as it is the foundation and root of all other numbers, as has been said. For this reason, all foundations—whether in man-made things, in natural things, or indeed even in divine things—are squared, as was declared in the preceding pages. It follows, then, that a division of a natural thing by the number four (which is the very order of Nature herself) is more excellent than a division by three or five; for those are secondary to the root of four and must, consequently, be placed after the quaternary. Finally, regarding the division of the earth into four parts, water into three, air into two, and fire into one: that distribution is not to be understood in the [literal] manner of an Author, as explained above, but rather in respect to the **formal proportion** <note>The ratio of "form" (light/spirit) to "matter" (bulk/heaviness) within an element.</note> in those elements. For I strive to demonstrate that the nature of earth—insofar as it is the base, and as it were the fountain and "cube" of matter—has little or no form or life-giving light within itself; it is like a vessel or "matrix" of Nature and a receptacle for celestial influences. Thus, what light it possesses is present more by accident than by its own nature. This is because it is furthest from the luminous source and is the coldest of all elements, specifically in the fourth degree; just as water is also cold, but in a more moderate degree. For this reason, it admits a single degree of light into itself; and so it goes for the others. It would be the mark of a wise man, therefore, to understand correctly before condemning thoughtlessly.

### TEXT XXII.

> *Again elsewhere, he subdivides every region of any Element or Heaven into three spaces: into the highest, middle, and lowest—not always following the probability of the senses. You see that his units are again arbitrary.*

### ANALYSIS.

We say that the likeness of the world is the image of the Archetype. Therefore, just as the Creator of all things expanded and revealed Himself by a procession from His unity into three properties <note>A reference to the Christian Trinity: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit.</note>, so that the world might be established according to their dispositions; so too did He divide the world into three regions, corresponding to the processions and properties of the most holy Trinity. The first of these is attributed to the Father, to whom the fountain and origin of light is assigned; thus, the first heaven or the work of the first day was given to the Father. The second heaven was given to the Son—whence it is said, *By the Word He established the heavens*—and the work of the third day, by which the elements of the lowest heaven were produced, corresponds to the Holy Spirit. According to the pattern of this reality, and according to this order, every heaven is again subdivided. This is proven because it was handed down by the most wise fathers of the <term>Theosophers</term> <note>Scholars of divine wisdom who sought to understand God through nature and revelation.</note> that the <term>Empyrean heaven</term> <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the universe, often imagined as a realm of pure fire or light.</note> is divided into three hierarchies. And you yourself were forced to admit that, according to the opinion of the Ancients, the ethereal region is distinct into nine spheres, which, when divided by three, leave three distinct parts. 

But he [Kepler] denies the equality of the intervals of the planetary spheres; and yet it will be more probable for me to affirm it than for him to deny it: therefore, let the dispute remain before the judge. In truth, he himself acknowledges that those same superior impressions exist "ideally" in lower things—as the "ideal" Zodiac is in the earth because it was "ideally" in the heaven; therefore, it is probable that the other portions of the celestial world also correspond to their impressions and, as it were, their images in the elements. In this, he also misunderstands me: for his imagination leads his concepts toward quantitative dimensions [physical size], while I was thinking of the proportions between matter and form in the regions of the world. I am not speaking, I say, of the dimensions of the intervals, but of the proportion of actions and passions between matter and form, or between water and light. He, therefore, focuses on the material pyramid and its quantitative dimensions; I was not even thinking of the material, except insofar as the "formal" was mixed with it. Thus, my quantities were ideal and proportional in a "formal" consideration, and not otherwise, although for the sake of demonstration I used mathematical lines and distances. Consequently, the probability of this dimension is not found in the senses, but rather in the intellect. You see, therefore, that his rejections are arbitrary and out of harmony with the truth of the matter.

### TEXT XXIII.

> *He nevertheless continues, because of this, to establish a <term>Diatessaron</term> <note>A musical interval of a fourth (ratio of 4:3).</note> between Earth and water; yet he applies its three intervals—tone, tone, and semitone—to three spaces (the highest, middle, and lowest). While the former have certain quantities determined by their causes, the latter do not even have boundaries set by nature; their measurements, according to these most general principles, are plainly indeterminate. And so it is with the rest. But to me, natural units are proposed: namely, the two extreme motions of the Planets (whether daily or hourly, it makes no difference), expressed by nature in their own certain quantities, in which I shall seek the Harmonies.*

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s systematic defense of his "World Harmony" against the criticisms of the astronomer Johannes Kepler. On the previous page, Fludd argued that the number four (the quaternary) is the fundamental root of all natural and divine structures. Here, he extends that logic to the physical layout of the earth and contrasts his "universal" motions with Kepler's "particular" astronomical calculations.</meta>

ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE.

### ANALYSIS.

Regarding the three regions of the earth, almost no one among the philosophers has ever doubted them, nor their differences, any more than they doubt the mineral, vegetable, and animal kingdoms which correspond to those regions of nature. Therefore, just as there is a difference of degree between the mineral and vegetable kingdoms, and likewise between the vegetable and animal—inasmuch as one is more fixed, earthy, watery, airy, or fiery than the other—so, without doubt, there are proportions between the activity of one region and another. 

We recognize, however, that just as minerals are more compact, fixed, and heavy, so too the portion of the earth tending toward the center is more ponderous. Similarly, just as vegetables are more volatile and naturally fluid and watery than minerals, so too the middle region of the earth abounds with the waters of springs, from which rivers flow. The superior part of the earth, however, is more like the animal nature; for in it the life of things abounds, and there is greater volatility and lightness. 

Just as there is a related difference between earth, water, and air, there is also a necessary concatenation <note>A chain-like connection or linking together.</note> in the proportions between the central, middle, and superficial regions of the earth. These proportions do not proceed from the nature of matter itself, but depend upon a hidden form <note>The "form" refers to the spiritual or light-based soul of the world that gives shape to raw matter.</note> acting invisibly upon it. Thus, they are not established by visual dimension or quantitative measurement, as that man <note>Referring to Johannes Kepler.</note> supposed; nor does my harmony consist in such [physical] proportions. Instead, it consists in the proportions between the more intense action or the relaxation of form, and the similar "passion" <note>In this context, "passion" means the capacity of matter to be acted upon or affected by the form.</note> of matter, by which one element in its whole and its parts differs from another. 

In this way, thick water imbued with saltiness is said to differ from sweet water, and sweet water again from a cloud, or a mist, or a thick vapor; and so on with the rest. His harmonic motions differ from mine in this way: the motions he embraces belong to the particular creatures of nature, whereas my motions are those of Nature herself, tending in a hidden manner toward perfection. His planetary motions are likewise more useful for distinguishing the seasons of the year (according to Genesis: *that they might be signs to measure times, days, and years*) and they have their own particular duties. My motions, however, are universal; by them, the nature of every thing and creature—whether elemental or celestial—is moved into alterations along with the elements themselves, and they are rotated continually by an appetite for perfection. For Nature always and incessantly tends toward the goal of perfection, even if she is sometimes hindered in her motion. This, I say, is the difference between the logic of his Harmony and mine:

### TEXT XXIV.

> *He seeks Harmonic proportions in the degrees of darkness and light, without respect to any motion: I seek Harmonies only in motions.*

### ANALYSIS.

That he indeed errs in this assertion can be seen from what has come before. For he himself thinks that I found my proportions by using geometric compasses, and he is deceived; for my Harmony consists in the truest and most certain actions of radiant nature, as has been said quite often. 

Furthermore, how ridiculous this assertion of his is becomes clear from the fact that light is a creature surpassing and excelling all others in activity. Therefore, since light and darkness are the subjects of my harmony, it is necessary that my Harmony consists in the motion and action of light upon the matter of darkness, as an agent acting upon a patient <note>The "agent" is the active force (light), while the "patient" is the thing receiving the action (darkness/matter).</note>. Consequently, that statement of the Author was very rash, and a manifest proof of envy, or at least of ignorance, wherein he relates that I seek harmonic proportions in degrees of darkness and light without respect to any motion. He looked upon the subject of my Harmony with his eyes, and not with his mind; thus, by grasping at the shadow and embracing the husk, he has utterly missed the substance and the kernel.

### TEXT XXV.

> *He tastes some few concordances and elicits them from a mixture of his pyramids—by which he carries around a world painted privately in his own mind—or tries to prove they are represented by it. I, however, have demonstrated that the entire body of Harmonic contemplation, with all its parts, exists in the extreme proper motions of the Planets, according to certain measures demonstrated by Astronomy. Thus, while his subjects are his own concepts of the world, for me the World itself, or the real motions of the Planets within it, are the subject of Mundane Harmony.*

<meta>Continuity: On the previous page, Fludd explained the three regions of the earth (central, middle, and superficial) and how they correspond to the physical and spiritual hierarchy of the universe. He now transitions into how these physical dimensions form a "World Monochord," contrasting his theory of harmony with that of Johannes Kepler.</meta>

### TO THE KEPPLERIAN APPENDIX.

### Analysis.

Although I have constructed my "monochord" <note>A single-stringed instrument used to demonstrate mathematical ratios in music.</note> from the axis of the pyramids—or, according to the author’s text in section 18, from the radius of the world-machine—that world monochord nevertheless examines the universal harmony of the world in no other way than my artificial musical monochord does. It tests all the world's intervals or differences in the harmonic "action and passion" <note>The interaction between the active spiritual form and the passive physical matter.</note> occurring between matter and form throughout the entire physical disposition of the world. In my musical monochord, all musical consonances and all intervals and proportions are explained—everything that can ever be comprehended in a musical system—by counting from its head to its tail. For, if the string is pressed in the middle, it will exhibit a double-octave consonance <note>original: "Diapason"</note> relative to the open string; and again, if any of those consonances are similarly divided into two parts, it will produce a quadruple proportion. This continues down to the ineffable division, as most clearly declared in the division of my monochord in Books 2 and 3 of my *Temple of Music*.

We say, therefore, that the same logic applies to a string one cubit long as to a string extended from the circumference of the world to the center of the world-machine regarding the discovery of harmonic intervals, proportions, and consonances. For if the entire radius of the world is divided into two parts, both of those parts will sound against the whole in a double proportion (an octave), just as was said regarding the artificial monochord. We say, furthermore, that **Art is the ape of Nature**; and this can be most rightly affirmed in artificial music, since it received its foundations from Nature herself. For there is no proportion or interval in voices or sounds that is not found in the physical constitution of the Great World <note>original: "Macrocosmi"</note>, which obtained the power of its Harmony from the perennial and supersubstantial source of Love. Thus, artificial music is a shadow or icon of the world’s harmony, and the world’s harmony is an image of that divine or Archetypal harmony <note>The "Archetype" is the original divine blueprint in the mind of God.</note>, which consists of the symphonic properties of the Trinity, encompassed by the number of the Tetractys <note>The "Tetractys" is a Pythagorean symbol of four rows of dots adding up to ten, representing the structural perfection of the universe.</note>.

Therefore, although few proportions of my Harmony may appear to the eye of the Author <note>Fludd is referring to Johannes Kepler.</note> to be contained in the string or the common axis of the two pyramids, the degrees of consonances and proportions explained in both the world monochord and the artificial one are nonetheless innumerable and ineffable. Indeed, I would dare to prove that every degree of the radial line, even when divided into atoms, has its own proper virtues in the world’s harmony; it will be impossible to distinguish this with the physical eyes, but very easy to demonstrate with the eyes of reason.

This Harmony of mine is not, therefore, so imaginary that it is merely depicted in my mind, since it consists of certain and infallible foundations, just as we can demonstrate in the artificial monochord. Indeed, our harmony is proven in the growth of vegetables, the generation and mutation of animals, and even in the constant logic of the elements. The Author’s theory, however, is likened to vain chimeras imagined in the air. It is extracted from the mere "accidents" <note>In philosophy, an "accident" is a non-essential quality of a thing.</note> of natural creatures—namely, from planetary motions—as if the entire soul of inferior things depended on the aspects or motions of the planets.

That this is entirely false is proven by an argument taken from Creation itself: as if, forsooth, it were certain that the earth did not exist, or that plants did not flourish upon it, or that seeds did not have their multiplication, and the other elements did not move by natural motion, before any of the planets (and consequently their aspects or motions) possessed any power or existence in the heavens! Yet those things were created with their creatures on the third day, while the planets were created only on a later day <note>According to Genesis, plants were created on Day 3, while the Sun, Moon, and stars were created on Day 4.</note>. And yet, he <note>Kepler.</note> admits no world harmony except from the motions of the planets. If this were granted to him, two absurdities would follow. The first is that there would be greater power in the "thing created" than in the "creator" (or in a natural object like a planet than in Nature herself). The second is that before the rising of the Sun and the planets, there was no harmony in the world, even though it was completed on the third day. Whether it is right or fair to say such a thing, I leave to your judgment, kind readers.

For if he himself teaches that the entire body of his harmonic contemplation, with all its parts, consists in the extreme motions of the planets, it follows that there was no Harmony in the world before the positioning of the planets in the heavens according to their intervals in Cubic, Tetrahedral, Dodecahedral, Icosahedral, and Octahedral proportions <note>These are the five "Platonic Solids" that Kepler used to explain the spacing between planetary orbits.</note>. This is because without distinct proportions of intervals, no Harmony can exist in the world. O, the extreme delirium of the man! He has forgotten the matter of light and darkness—indeed, the royal governing faculty of Nature itself—and like a pagan worshipper of idols <note>original: "idololattræ"</note>, he flees to the stars (the mere buildings of nature) and determines that the action and governance of the world's structure flow from their essences. He neglects that true morning star, the very Carbuncle of Wisdom, which must be placed many degrees above the sun and the celestial stars in beauty and clarity. He, therefore, establishes the celestial sun as the source of motion everywhere in his works; but I establish Wisdom itself as the spring flowing everywhere through the world.

If my opinion, though consistent with the sacred text, seems unlikely or unclear to some, let them (I say) enjoy the trophy of ignorance which has been fixed for many centuries. Let these words of mine—which I have brought forth truly and from the fountain of truth—be vain to a world overwhelmed by ignorance. But I know there is a chorus of the wise who will determine that the world and its harmony, published in my manner, is not something merely depicted or fantastical, but is very close to reality and truth. They will not judge my concept of the world to be [merely] my harmony...

ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE

...my subject, but the Soul of the World <note>original: "animam mundi"</note> itself, and its motion within its watery body; indeed, with eyes wide open, they will perceive that the Author <note>Fludd refers to Kepler here.</note> himself has incongruously accepted accidents <note>In philosophy, an "accident" is a non-essential quality, like color or weight, as opposed to the "substance" or essence of a thing.</note> as substance—namely, choosing private motion instead of Luminous Nature herself as the foundation of his harmony.

### TEXT XXVI.

> *From these few points, I believe it is clear that although the most profound philosophy which Robert [Fludd] hands down requires a knowledge of harmonic proportions to understand its dense mysteries, nevertheless, he who has learned my entire work would still be quite far from those most perplexed mysteries, just as those mysteries themselves have receded from the most accurate certainty of mathematical demonstrations.*

### ANALYSIS.

Now our friend Johannes here, with this distinguished publication of his victory, desires to put an end to this Appendix of his, in which he thinks to build a lasting monument to his own praises. He proposes to thrust my reputation, such as it is, into the abyss of disgrace and to plunge it into the whirlpool of ignorance and lack of skill, so that his triumph over my world might appear more glorious to Fortune. And indeed, let him enjoy the trophy he hoped to plant, if he can. But truly, it will nevertheless be fair that the truth holds its place, and falsehood be exiled into the polar darkness.

Regarding the mysteries, he finally speaks of opening those which he says were poorly understood by me, as if I lacked demonstrations; meanwhile, he himself has already brought to light the *Macrocosmic Mystery* <note>Kepler’s *Mysterium Cosmographicum*, published in 1596, which Fludd notes was 22 years prior.</note> which he admits was done twenty-two years ago. There, he seems to acknowledge—though fruitlessly—that the whole mystery of Nature lies hidden. But because I remember that there is a double mystery in the world, namely of truth and of injustice (as we gather from the Book of Revelation), we must see whether his mystery is one tending toward delusion, or toward a vision of truth; toward a vain and false philosophy (which the Apostle Paul mentions) or a true one.

Indeed, he confesses that the force of his mystery is contained in this subject of his Harmony. What, then, shall we judge of it, when the foundations found in this work seem to differ so far from the truth? He judges my Harmonic proportions, in his usual manner, to have receded from the most accurate certainty of mathematical demonstrations because he is more practiced in "vulgar" mathematics <note>Fludd distinguishes between "vulgar" math (mere counting and measuring) and "formal" math (the study of the divine archetypes and spiritual realities).</note>; but of that natural and truly formal mathematics, he seems never to have sipped or tasted. Since this is the case (as appears through his inquiry made in this Appendix), a Wise Man will deem it impossible for such a person to judge colors correctly, who not only is ignorant of the effects of light and darkness in the machine of the world but does not even hesitate to deny them with a hardened face.

But truly, as far as my mathematical proportions are concerned—which I published through demonstrations to explain my intentions regarding Nature—I know them to be as true and certain as I know for a fact that he has wandered from the true path regarding my intent and mind in the hidden knowledge of nature, or the mystery of light and water. Nor indeed (let him say whatever he pleases) can he find any fault in those demonstrations of mine. But here lies the whole difficulty: he devises the external motions of a natural thing, while I consider the internal and essential acts flowing from nature itself. He holds the tail, while I embrace the head; I notice the principal cause, while he notices its effects. And yet, although his extreme motions are "real" (as he says), he is stuck more in the mud and mire of the impossibility of his own doctrine and bound by the most perplexed chains of obscurity than he can easily free himself from those snares with his honor intact, or redeem his understanding for the smallest price. And so, he who made a pit for others has ignorantly fallen into it himself.

### Examination, fulfilling the place of the number 27, or the Ternary Cube.

Now, so that we may complete the perfect cube of the number three <note>3 x 3 x 3 = 27. Fludd often structures his arguments using sacred geometry and numerology.</note> in this Appendix of ours—namely, the 27th place—let us see, I pray, in a very few words, what the discrepancy is between the core of his Harmonic tension and the subject of my harmony: and finally, let us inquire which of them has more testimonies of truth, supported by philosophical reasons and authorities drawn from the Sacred Fountain, to weave into their doctrine.

### Epitome of Johannes Kepler's Harmony.

The subject of Johannes Kepler's Harmony consists, first, in the proportional position of the Planets in the heavens. Second, for the completion of his harmony, he places the Earth itself among the number of the moving stars of heaven. Third, he makes the Sun that immobile center—and yet the source of motion—around which he says all the Planets move, except for the Moon. He derives the proportion of the positions of any Planets from each other (by which each sounds a consonance different from the rest) from Pythagorean, Aristotelian, Platonic, and Eu-
