<meta>Continuity: Fludd continues his critique of Johannes Kepler’s work, shifting here to a detailed summary of Kepler’s own cosmological model—specifically how Kepler maps the five Platonic solids and musical harmonies to the planetary orbits—before Fludd offers his own counter-arguments.</meta>

# TO THE KEPLERIAN APPENDIX:

...derived from Euclidean, Proclean, and Copernican doctrines, he has calculated the proportions of the five regular geometric solids; <note>The "five regular solids" are the Platonic solids: the cube, tetrahedron, octahedron, dodecahedron, and icosahedron.</note> although he admits that his discovery surpasses the intentions of Pythagoras, Aristotle, or Plato. For whereas they applied the proportion of the **Cube** to the earth, this author of ours attributes the **Cube** to **Saturn**, and establishes its proportion as the interval between the orbits of Saturn and Jupiter. They also gave the **Tetrahedron** <note>A four-sided pyramid.</note> to fire, but he gives it to **Mars**, and grants its proportion to the distance between the orbits of Jupiter and Mars. Furthermore, they attributed the **Dodecahedron** <note>A twelve-sided solid.</note> to the heavens, but he has fitted its proportion to the space between the orbits of Mars and the Earth. From there, they made the **Icosahedron** <note>A twenty-sided solid.</note> a symbol of the element of water, but he appropriated its nature to **Venus**, and to the distance between the orbits of the Earth and Venus. Finally, the figure of the **Octahedron** <note>An eight-sided solid.</note>, which they applied to Air, he ascribes to **Mercury** and its proportional dimension between the orbits of Venus and Mercury. The reasons why such confidence took hold of him, that he would so twist the sayings of the Philosophers to his own opinion, he seems to explain with these words:

*Preface to Book 1: When they gave the Cube to the earth, perhaps they understood Saturn, whose orbit is removed by the interposition of Jupiter; for Saturn was allotted a movement so slow it is nearest to rest. Thus Aristotle read that the Octahedron was given to Air, when they perhaps understood Mercury, as it is the swiftest of all, just like the air itself. By the word Fire, perhaps Mars was suggested; and so in the rest.* <note>original: "Prooem. lib. 1. Cum ipsi Cubum terrae dabant forte Saturnum intellexerunt... Ignis vocabulo fortasse Mars fuit insinuatus: & sic in caeteris."</note>

So he says. He even gathers from the opinion of Proclus <note>A 5th-century Neoplatonist philosopher.</note> that the Pythagoreans themselves held a reciprocal opinion—namely, that those five solids are not assigned to elements, but to the Planets themselves; Proclus teaches that the sole end of Geometry is to teach how the heavens received shapes suitable to certain of their parts. Another pillar by which he seems to support this opinion of his is found on *page 59, Book 2*, where he speaks of the five solids of the Pythagoreans in these words: *I said in the preamble of my book that it seemed consistent to me that this was also the doctrine of the Ancients, but hidden according to the custom of the sect.* <note>original: "Dixi in praeambulo libri mei, mihi videri consaneaneum, eandem doctrinam etiam Veterum fuisse, sed occultatam more sectae."</note>

How these solids are adapted with their angles to the intervals of the orbits of any Planet, he indicates more expressly in *Chapter 3, Book 5*. But, so that these things might better agree among themselves, and so that his harmony might be more symphonic, he deemed it necessary to count the **Earth** among the stars. *His discourse is of this kind in Chapter 3, Book 5: But because among the common crowd of students it is still new, and a dogma most absurd to the hearing of many, that the Earth is one of the Planets and is carried among the stars around an immobile Sun, therefore let them know there that, according to the opinion of Copernicus, the earth measures a circle...* <note>original: "At, quia apud vulgus studiosorum adhuc novum est... Terram ex Planetis unum esse... idcirco sciant ibi, quod, juxta Copernici sententiam, tellus circulum metiatur..."</note> [He also states] that these [circles] also find a place in the hypotheses of Tycho Brahe; <note>Tycho Brahe (1546–1601) proposed a model where the Sun orbits the Earth, but all other planets orbit the Sun.</note> furthermore, that this author [Kepler] holds everything else in common with Copernicus regarding the arrangement and contemplation of the bodies. Only the Copernican annual motion of the earth does he himself transfer into the whole system of planetary orbits, in which the Sun, which occupies its center, is by the consensus of both authors. 

So he says. He also says there that, according to the opinion of Copernicus (for whom in *Chapter 3, Book 5* he establishes the real motion of its body walking the middle path between the outer circles of Mars and the inner circles of Venus), and finally regarding the Sun, he seems in the same chapter (following the opinion of Copernicus, for in *Chapter 7* he establishes the <term>retrogradations and stations<gloss>The appearance of planets moving backward or standing still in the sky due to the Earth's relative motion.</gloss></term> as a fantasy) to persuade everyone to apply the same, leaving behind the ancient astronomical hypotheses of Ptolemy, etc. In *Chapter 4, Book 5*, he establishes that the Earth, along with the remaining Planets (for in *Chapter 7* he establishes the Planets besides the Sun), with only the Moon excepted, is carried around the immobile Sun. But he makes the Moon's motion irregular compared to the rest, since he makes its center the Earth, just as the center of the Earth and the remaining Planets is the Sun itself. 

Therefore, he makes **Saturn** and **Jupiter**—between which there is the interval of the **Cube**, just as between Jupiter and **Mars** there is the distance of the **Tetrahedron**; similarly Mars and the **Earth**, between which the space of the **Dodecahedron** is expressed, just as between the Earth and **Venus** there is the distance of the **Icosahedron**; and finally Venus and **Mercury**, between which the dimension of the **Octahedron** is interposed—he makes them all circle the immobile Sun and move above its center, from which they receive their motion. Whence in *Axiom 4, Chapter 9, Book 5* and in many other places, he makes the **Sun the source of motion**. 

From these proportions of the regular solids to one another (of which any one is taken from another like a son from a father, and the lesser from the greater, as the Tetrahedron from the Cube, and the Dodecahedron from the Tetrahedron, and the Icosahedron from the Dodecahedron, and the Octahedron as if from the center of all, and hence he says one is extracted from another by a certain harmonic proportion), he strives to compose a harmony arising proportionally from the variety of the <term>eccentric motions<gloss>Orbits that are not perfectly circular but off-center.</gloss></term> of the Planets. And first, he shows various degrees of kinship of harmonic proportions with the five regular figures in *Chapter 2, Book 5*. Hence, in *Chapter 9, Proposition 8*, he shows that because of the proportion of the Cube, there are attributed to Saturn and Jupiter the ratios **1:2 to 1:3**, that is, the **Octave** <note>original: "Diapason"</note> and the **Octave-and-a-Fifth** <note>original: "Diapente epi-Diapason"</note>, and that the joined proper proportions of the extreme motions have about a **2:3** ratio, or **Perfect Fifth** <note>original: "Diapente"</note>. To Venus and Mercury, he attributes a greater harmony of **1:4**, the **Double Octave** <note>original: "Disdiapason"</note>; to Jupiter and Mars, nearly **1:8**, a **Triple Octave** <note>original: "Trisdiapason"</note>. And so in the others: and this indeed is the force of his purely celestial Harmony. 

But he composes another "mixed" harmony from the variety of the <term>aspects<gloss>The angular relationships between planets as seen from Earth, such as conjunction or opposition.</gloss></term> of these Planets so arranged, making angles by their radiations upon the earth. In this species of Harmony, he seems to return to the proportion of regular figures in a plane, just as in the celestial harmony he follows the directions of the same in the solid. This, unless I am mistaken, is the subject of his entire book, and the foundation of his doctrine.

D

### ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE:
### An Epitome of my Physics and Worldly Harmony.

From unity—whether you call it divine power or the primeval darkness—I produce duality: namely light, which is the universal form in action, and water, which is the matter and the passive principle. I believe that this duality—the root of the world—expanded through multiplication and dilated into its own square, procreating the four distinct elements of the world that fill the entire hollow of the cosmic fabric. The earthy dregs of the darkness within the capacity of the world, in the manner of a frightful shadow or a deformed chaos perturbing all things from every side, were reflected back into the center of the world's circumference by the power of divine light; this center I call Earth. 

I also say that there is a pyramidal proportion within the watery nature, whose base is in the earth, while its cone <note>the "apex" or point of the pyramid</note> tends upward toward the periphery like the flame of a fire. I assert that in this physical pyramid, a continuous alteration from the coarse to the subtle occurs infallibly because of the unequal proportions of the invisible light lurking within the worldly matter, producing various actions and hidden motions. I make these acts of nature the foundations of my Worldly Harmony, and not **quantitative measurements**, as Johannes <note>Johannes Kepler</note> supposed. 

Finally, I make a third progression from the number four <note>the quaternary</note> to the number eight <note>the octonary</note>, so that at last, by this **Cubic** dimension, I may arrive at those things composed of the fourfold number. Beyond this degree, there is no natural progress in this world. I say that in this alteration, the most exact harmony of Nature in both birth and decay <note>generation and corruption</note> is apparent daily, even to the blind: namely, a progression from the number four to the **Cube** (or eight) during birth, and a regression from the **Cube** back to the square (or four) during decay. This is the composition from the elements through birth, and the resolution from that composition back into the elements—a movement backward from the finish to the start. Thus, all things after creation take on the cycle of birth and decay. For I affirm this to be the **Wheel of Nature** constantly turning in the world, according to that saying of the Philosopher: *The generation of one thing is the corruption of another.* <note>original: "Generatio unius est alterius corruptio." A classic Aristotelian principle of physics.</note> 

Yet the permanent root of these things is always in the same state of action, possessing the privilege of creation, so that from it the four elements are preserved in their being: namely, **Light** and **Water**, which are the foundations of the world. I conclude, therefore, that the "harmony of the pyramids" is that which is made by the ratio between the root, the duality, and the square—that is, between light and water and the four elements which arise from them, just as a square arises from its root. Therefore, the elements are nourished and preserved in their being and essence by a certain harmonic relationship proceeding from the principal roots. Their active nature (the harmonic form) is administered to the elements in proportion, and this act is the true wisdom of the world; while the passive nature, sounding in accord with the form in a fit and convenient measure, grants the succession of matter to them. The Harmony of **Birth, Growth, Decay, and Decrease** occurs by a reciprocal ratio held between the square and the Cube. And this is the final degree of Harmony, whose first and primordial degree was between unity (divine power) and duality, and whose second was between duality and the number four. This, in brief, is the subject of my Physical Philosophy and the foundations of my worldly harmony.

---

->A Comparison of my Worldly Harmony with that of Johannes Kepler;<-
->where it is declared in what they agree, and in<-
->which they differ.<-

Johannes Kepler deduces the origin of his Harmony from the five regular bodies of mathematics <note>The Platonic Solids</note>: namely, the **Cube, Tetrahedron, Dodecahedron, Icosahedron, and Octahedron**, following the opinion of the Ancient Philosophers. This is also the subject of my Harmony, although instead of those **Geometrical** terms, I have used **Physical** words. Regarding the subject, therefore, we agree in this: that he twists the five Geometric bodies of the Pythagoreans, Platonists, and Aristotle himself away from their proper meaning to suit his own, dragging them in, as it were, by the hair. He offers this excuse in the *Preface to Book 1*: "When they compared the **Cube** to the earth, perhaps they understood **Saturn** in place of the earth," and so on for the rest. In this way, by misapplying suppositions and imaginations, he laid the fabulous foundations of his Harmony. 

Furthermore, in *Proposition 25 of Book 2*, it seems to me he asserts as a settled matter that this same doctrine (meaning the relationship of the 5 solids to the intervals of the planetary orbits) was also held by the Ancients, but hidden in the manner of their Sect. Thus, he appears inconsistent and uncertain regarding the intentions of the Philosophers. Yet he reveals their minds using words plainly contrary to his own when he says: *Pythagoras, Plato, and Aristotle called these "worldly figures" insofar as they regarded the five simple bodies of the world.* From this, they made the **Cube** a symbol of earth, the **Icosahedron** of water, the **Octahedron** of air, the **Tetrahedron** of fire, and the **Dodecahedron** of the heavens. How poorly Johannes has applied these bodies to his own intentions will be very easy to discern for anyone who considers that the entire world was completed on the third day of creation, before the Sun and stars were placed in the heavens. For on the third day, the **Elements** were completed and ordered according to the laws of nature...

### TO THE KEPLERIAN APPENDIX.

<margin>
The act of the world, or the world, is seen—it wills—in earth, in earth and water—from every act, and [the world] is produced from the number—the melted or perfected thing—also on the Eighth [day]—this—only as—at the creation—it is said. Through the water—the square—is indicated—but to where—the harmonic—triple—standing—below—is social—number—harmony.
</margin>

...and on the second day the heaven was completed. From this, one may see that if they <note>the ancient philosophers</note> applied these geometric figures to the five cosmic bodies <note>The five Platonic solids: tetrahedron, cube, octahedron, dodecahedron, and icosahedron</note>, they undoubtedly did not even think about the distances of the stars from each other; for these stars were not created before the completion of the world. Therefore, Kepler’s opinion regarding the intentions of the Philosophers is incongruous, and consequently, the foundations of his "Worldly Harmony" are very weak. 

But in truth, the intention of the Philosophers agrees in all respects with the subject of my own Harmony. For my opinion holds that from Chaos, or <term>Hyle</term> <note>original: "Hyle"; a Greek term for "primeval matter" used in alchemy and philosophy to describe the raw substance of the universe</note>—the opaque and dark matter—light emerged with watery matter, as if from a mother’s womb. This birth is best explained through the drawing out of the Tetrahedron <note>the pyramid shape</note> (representing fire or primary light) from the cube, which is the largest and most opaque body <note>representing earth or solidity</note>. Does he not seem to acknowledge this very same thing in these words from Chapter 1 of his 5th book: *The Cube is the outermost and most ample, because it is the first-born and contains the logic of the whole?* <note>original: "Cubus extremus est & amplissimus, quia primigenius & rationem habens totius?"</note> What does this have to do with Saturn or its orbit, which is the smallest part of the whole? 

We say, therefore, that just as the Tetragon <note>a four-sided figure</note>, which is also a Body, was born from the first-born Cube through a first and virgin birth, so also the formal light first appeared out of the darkness with its watery body or "fecund matter," and this was the work of the first day. For thus, according to the Book of <term>Ecclesiasticus</term> <note>original: "Ecclesiastici"; referring to the Wisdom of Sirach 1:4</note>, *Wisdom was created before all things,* and thus duality was created in unity, having within itself a triangular force, through which the Trinity in unity worked in the world, retaining within itself the power of the tetragon, or the four universal elements. 

Then one arrives at the second work of creation, in which the Dodecahedron <note>a 12-sided solid</note> is extracted from the depths of the Tetrahedron. For, once the spirit of light was made on the first day, the heaven—or the "expanse"—was created from its depths on the second day. Because this was the fifth element <note>the "quintessence" or aether</note>, its symbol is composed of pentagons; and the number of angles of one plane of the Tetrahedron, when multiplied by its sides, produced the sides of the heavens expressed through the figure of the Dodecahedron. For this reason, it is right to believe that the eighth heaven <note>the sphere of the fixed stars</note> was divided into twelve places by reason of its belt, which is called the Zodiac. 

The work of the third day, however, is the gathering of the waters into their place; their symbol is the Icosahedron <note>a 20-sided solid</note>, from whose belly the hidden air immediately flew out, whose symbol is the Octahedron <note>an 8-sided solid</note>. For air is made from water by refining it. The residue of the dark Cube, however, settled into the earth and now holds the name of "fertile earth," which was previously called by Moses the *formless earth* <note>original: "terra inanis"; from Genesis 1:2</note>. Therefore, our Physical history of the world introduces all these things, though it accepts physical terms instead of mathematical ones for the sake of the subject. 

Is this not all contained in the words of <term>Hermes Trismegistus</term> <note>original: "Mercurij Trismegisti"; the legendary Hellenistic figure associated with the "Hermetic" wisdom that Fludd champions</note> in Sermon 3? 
*There was an infinite shadow in the abyss; moreover, water and an intellectual spirit were within the chaos through divine power. But a holy splendor flourished, which led out the elements from beneath the sand and the moist nature.* And when the light things had been undefined, they soon flew up into the high region, while the heavy things settled beneath the sand. Once things were distinguished and weighed—those things which were carried by a fiery spirit—the heaven shone forth in seven circles. 

By "infinite shadow," he understands (speaking in a Geometric manner) the Cube, beyond which there is no progression in the regular bodies; it is the first-born and most opaque body. This body, when it was "pregnant," was called Chaos, and it gave birth to water with the spirit of light. By its power and action, the universal water of the heaven and the elements were produced in order—namely, one from another—and all things, carried on fiery wings, were distinguished; and everything that was foul and heavy from the cube settled beneath the moist sand. 

The wise man acknowledged that all this was done before the Planets were established in the celestial palace, as he seems to indicate with these words: *Once things were distinguished and weighed, which were carried by a fiery spirit*—here he teaches that the fiery spirit is the reason for the Music and the worldly harmony—*the heaven shone forth in seven circles.* 

By these words, he clearly seems to teach that the worldly harmony was completed *before* the seven Planets appeared in the sky. Consequently, it is not the motions of the seven Planets, but that divine and fiery spirit revealed out of Chaos—distinguishing and weighing all things in the scales of justice, governing and sustaining every region of the world by its power, which existed before all things and was the first-born, and thus existed before the existence of the Planets—this, I say, gives life, Harmony, and concord to the elements and things composed of elements, and by no means do the Planets or their motions do so. 

We conclude, therefore, that his <note>Kepler's</note> five bodies truly belong to my Harmony and to the intentions of the Ancient Philosophers. He, however, uses them in a "spurious" or false way, and entirely against the thoughts of the said Philosophers, who undoubtedly had the mirror of truth before their eyes. In that mirror, they were instructed in the truth of that matter, and not without the most accurate reason did they adapt these regular bodies to its various changes and parts. 

In other matters, except for the common basics of Music, we certainly agree on little or almost nothing. For he follows Copernicus <note>the sun-centered model</note>, while I do not disdain to embrace the ancient teaching of Ptolemy <note>the earth-centered model</note>. He desires to measure nature and its unities by the identity of lines and captures worldly Harmony through mathematical dimensions. I, however, attribute my natural harmony to the hidden actions of *form* within worldly matter. He establishes the planetary motions as the foundations of Harmony; I ascribe the power of my Harmony to the motion of the fiery spirit, swimming from the beginning everywhere through the "liquid" of the world <note>the aetherial or watery expanse</note> and stirring it into a diversity of harmonic proportions.

And so we see that there is very little consensus between us, precisely because he abuses the true principles of the Philosophers and turns them toward an alien meaning, while I bind myself as exactly as possible to the
->sayings of the Philosophers and the testimony of the sacred scriptures.<-

Fludd aligns his "Physical Harmony" with ancient Hermetic wisdom and the Book of Genesis, contrasting his Ptolemaic, spirit-driven model with Kepler's Copernican, math-driven one.</summary>

<page-num>40</page-num>
->DISCURS. ANALYT.<-

### Testimonies and authorities, both Philosophical and Divine, confirming or refuting the Harmony of Johannes Kepler.

In the first place, he <note>Johannes Kepler</note> made the five regular bodies of Geometry the basis of his Harmony. Next, he established the motion of the Planets as the cause of his harmony, which he seems to make twofold: namely, from radiations onto the earth making various angles there (about which he writes throughout book 4), or from the motion of the planets' eccentricity <note>the deviation of an orbit from a perfect circle</note> or change in distance from the Sun (about which he writes everywhere in book 5). And indeed, to raise this Harmony upon more secure foundations, he establishes the Earth as one of the Planets, and teaches that it is carried among the stars around the immobile Sun. He calls the Sun the center of the orbits of the planets—Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Earth, Venus, and Mercury—and consequently calls it the center of the world in Book 4, chapter 9. However, he wants the Moon to have the Earth for its center. Finally, he seems to assert that the intervals of the harmonic differences of these motions, by which the consonances <note>musical harmonies</note> are composed, took their origin from various proportions of the five regular bodies of Geometry. For the "harmony of aspects" <note>original: "harmonia aspectuum"; the astrological relationship between planets based on their angular distance</note>, he uses his own invention; to confirm this, in chapter 7 of book 4, he makes the Earth an animal, and attributes to it a peculiar soul, which he says—ignited and irritated by the angles of planetary radiations—is accustomed to stir up rains, storms, and whatever meteors <note>In the 17th century, "meteors" referred to any atmospheric phenomenon, including rain, wind, and lightning</note> in the regions of the air.

In the Holy Scriptures, no arguments are found for the wandering stars <note>planets</note> or aspects to stir up storms and meteors; rather, they acknowledge that the Sun and Moon were created on the fourth day for this use: that the Sun should bring daylight and rule the day, while the Moon with the stars should perform this same duty for the night; likewise, that they should be signs for fixed times, for days, and for years (Genesis 1). And Psalm 104:19: "He appointed the moon for fixed seasons; the sun knows its going down." And Psalm 136:8: "God made the sun to obtain dominion in the night." <note>Fludd appears to be paraphrasing or using a variant text; standard biblical texts assign the Sun to the day and Moon to the night</note>. Therefore, he makes no mention here of aspects, by whose harmony so many alterations are said to occur here on earth, but rather of the aforementioned uses. From this, he indicates that all the stars, depending on the Sun and Moon themselves, have no more power than that for which they were made from the beginning. I confess indeed with the most learned Philosophers—namely the Platonist Orpheus—that the Sun is most rightly called the "Right Eye of the World"; and that Heraclitus said, not without wit, that the Sun is like the Soul of the World; seeing as if it were removed, no vital state or breath would remain in it. Some even wish it to be called the "Heart of Heaven," because just as in the heart there exists a living fountain of blood, moistening and watering the other members of the human body and infusing vital motion, so in the Sun there seems to be the vegetation and preservation of all things, both lower and higher, insofar as it is said to disperse heat and life everywhere by its light. They make a reason of this kind: because light is a simple act, turning all things to itself with life-giving heat, permeating all beings, and leading their qualities and virtues through all things, while dispersing shadows and darkness. And hence they say that Phoebus <note>a classical name for the Sun</note> sits in the middle, shining with golden hair, and holds the scepter like a king and emperor of the world. Iamblichus and Proclus <note>Neoplatonic philosophers</note> affirm that all the virtues of the heavenly beings are present in him, and for this reason they believe the Soul of the World exists in the Sun, and they established that sphere of equality or of the soul through which this soul passes.

Although, I say, all these things may be rightly raised—and furthermore, that this same doctrine was published in our book on the *Macrocosm* <note>original: "Macrocosmo"; referring to Fludd's own work *Utriusque Cosmi Historia*</note>, namely concerning the admirable effect of the Sun, and its position and motion by reason of the intersections of pyramids <note>Fludd’s theory that the universe is shaped by two intersecting pyramids, one of light/spirit and one of darkness/matter</note>, and many other things—nevertheless, I wish the diligent reader to rightly grasp and understand the sense of the Philosophers' words and my own: namely, that all these things should be understood concerning the *visible* Sun, than which (regarding sense and sensible effects) nothing in this world can be judged more worthy or noble. But in truth, if we look a little more accurately with rational eyes into the sayings of the Philosophers, we will perceive that there is a certain **invisible Sun** of the world moving everywhere, which surpasses the visible Sun in power as much as the soul surpasses the body, or the spiritual the material. Hence indeed, just as Plato used to call the celestial Sun the "visible son of God," so also the Holy Scriptures call Wisdom (moving and working invisibly everywhere in the world) the "invisible emanation of God." Wherefore the wiser of the Philosophers fell into this opinion: "From God," they say, "something nearest to Him arises, namely Nature herself, which is that invisible fire from which Zoroaster and Heraclitus of Ephesus taught that all things were begotten." And in Ecclesiasticus 1, we find that Wisdom was created first. And this is that fiery force which the Spirit of the Lord—which is fiery love—brought forth when it was carried over the waters; without its vigor, nothing can be generated or endure. This is that inspiration inspired into created things by God, when it was said at the birth of the world, "Increase and multiply." This, I say, invisibly bright Nature is that Sun which was not made on the fourth day, but from the beginning, by whose power the world was completed through the mediation of the Word before any visible Sun was established in the heavens; which excels the visible Sun in power as much as the spiritual and invisible Son of God excels that visible one of heaven. Everywhere this secret Sun is present, and supplies life to anyone; it is present to all night and day, without which nothing can live or subsist. Happy indeed is he who can uncover or reveal that Nature; for by its presence, the mysteries of the whole world are revealed. And indeed, although this Wisdom-Light is hidden in itself and...

He argues that while the visible Sun is the "Heart of Heaven," it is merely a physical manifestation of a primordial, spiritual Light or Wisdom created before the stars.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s critique of Johannes Kepler. While the previous page discussed Kepler’s physical and geometric basis for planetary harmony, Fludd here begins to argue for a "supernatural" source of that harmony, distinguishing between the visible, physical Sun and an "Invisible Sun" of divine Wisdom.</meta>

# TO THE KEPPLERIAN APPENDIX
->D 3<-
<meta>catchword: Plane-</meta>

[the light is] invisible, yet because of the most suitable reception of its rays by a body well-polished and perfectly disposed for this purpose, it appears to our eyes through its effect. Through the reflection of these rays—as if from a well-prepared mirror adapted to gather infinite rays into a single center—a heat is excited that is most certainly felt by the touch. Such, therefore, is the composition of the solar body above all other stellar bodies; because of the extreme purity of its substance and its unified polish, it is granted solely to reveal the beam of the **Invisible Sun** to worldly eyes, although not in the exact manner in which it exists in its divine essence. 

And, just as we perceive that a unified force is stronger, and for this reason the invisible rays of the Sun itself moving in the air burn or heat from a distance when collected into a single point of a **burning mirror**, so too by the same reasoning does a portion of the rays of the **Invisible Sun** (which are dispersed everywhere) penetrate into the center of the solar body. This body is a subject best disposed to receive such a noble form; by gathering them into a single point or center, it first visibly illuminates the world from its heart, and by its circular reverberation, it emits everywhere more subtle and visible acts and rays. 

Hence, this whole power is wrongly attributed to the celestial sun merely because the senses perceive the rays of light and heat deriving from it, and not from **Wisdom** <note>original: "Sapientia"; Fludd equates divine Wisdom with the source of all light</note> lurking everywhere in the world (though invisibly due to its purity). Wisdom is the source of solar light and the life of every thing. It is as if someone were to say that a burning mirror is the origin of fire and heat, and not the sun itself. 

From this it is clear that the solar body is like an instrument of that Wisdom, by which it renders its effects in the world more sensible. Indeed, its rays and lucid nature delight in the sun and are gathered there in a certain way to cherish inferior creatures with its heat, but especially to rule during the day, just as the Moon (which Philosophers call *the left eye of the world*) rules with the stars during the night. 

Therefore, because of this so ample "creeping-forth" of divine light from the lucid source—that is, the fiery Spirit of Wisdom—and its so copious presence in the Sun, it is said that *the Lord placed his tabernacle in the Sun* <note>A reference to Psalm 19:4</note>. We acknowledge that the Sun of the heaven is held to be, and truly is, one of the more outstanding creatures of the world, insofar as we know that God claims any thing for Himself only to the extent that it is capable of containing light. 

The visible Sun, therefore, possesses its nobility from that invisible one, which the Kabbalists call the angel **Metattron** <note>original: "Metattron"; a high-ranking mediator or celestial scribe in Jewish mystical tradition</note>, or the **soul of the Messiah**, or the power of the **Tetragrammaton** <note>original: "virtutem Tetragrammati"; the four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH</note> (which they made the *soul of the world*), and which the Sacred Scriptures call **Wisdom**, or the brightness and pure emanation of the power of God. But truly, since that which creates a quality is greater than the quality itself, it follows that the Wisdom which creates the luminous power of the Sun is more noble and bright than the Sun itself. 

Wherefore, since *the whole world (as the Sage says in Wisdom 1) is filled with His Spirit*, no less than the heaven, why should we not attribute the harmony of the elements and elemental things to the "Principle" <note>the Creator/Source</note> rather than to the "Principled" <note>the created result</note>? For He works every moment and incessantly, albeit invisibly, and labors with His fiery love toward the generations, corruptions, mutations, alterations, and rotations of the elements and those things composed of them.

We acknowledge, indeed, that there is a very great power in the celestial Sun; but we attribute all of it to the unique **Soul of the World** <note>original: "anima mundi"</note> operating as invisibly in the world as it does visibly in the Sun. Thus, by the gathering of light and the fiery nature expanded through the air, we see terrestrial fire and flame, and indeed lightning and flashes produced, which then vanish again by spreading out. And the more skillful of the Philosophers acknowledged this when they cast this saying: *There is nothing in the very circle of the firmament, besides the soul, that bears a greater likeness to God than the Sun and its light.* By this they seem to argue that the invisible soul of the world is far more noble than the Sun or its light. 

And although the nobility of this subject and the sublimity of divine nature persuaded me to weave these thoughts with a more polished thread of speech, nevertheless, according to my custom (for such is the "thinness of my wit," I confess), I am forced to proceed here in "pedestrian speech" <note>plain, unadorned prose</note> to explain the reason and origin of the creature's power to the world. I do this so that I may reveal the difference between the Heathen idolaters of this world—who are accustomed to worship the host of heaven while neglecting the true God—and the ancient Christians, who adore the true Wisdom or the power of the Tetragrammaton. 

This, then, is the title we can attribute to the visible Sun: namely, that it is the **visible seat of the soul of the world**, just as the whole mundane spirit is the **invisible seat** of its universal soul. Consequently, the **Harmony of the World** proceeds from that soul or primary Lucid Nature—namely, Wisdom, which is the first act of the world—and not from a secondary source alone, except insofar as Nature is accustomed to accelerate its effects in that "palace" of hers. For Nature is like a hidden blower <note>like one who pumps the bellows of an organ</note>, the Sun is like the **Organ**, and it is the world's principal instrument and pipe through which the sensible Harmony is seen and perceived by its effect. However, she uses infinite other imperceptible instruments in these lower regions to perfect her operations in the world, as can be seen throughout the sacred letters and will appear more broadly in what follows.

But, to descend now to the specific **Soul of the Earth**, we say that its soul is that very same one which is the **Soul of the World** and the **Spirit of God**, as has been said, *filling the orb*, or the *power of the Tetragrammaton present everywhere*. Hence it is said in *Psalm 104:32: "When God looked at the earth it trembled, and when He touched the mountains they smoked."* Was this because of the commotion and irradiation of the Earthly soul due to some planetary aspect, or by immediate divine agitation? *When the earth opened itself and swallowed Dathan (Psalm 106:17), when it was moved and trembled (2 Samuel 21), when it suffered floods from rains*—were these accidents of hers from the motions of the stars, or from the divine spirit, which cherishes and disturbs all things by its own motion? It is said in *Job 9:5: that God moved the earth out of its place, so that its pillars trembled, and He transferred mountains, but not the aspects* <note>original: "aspectus"; Fludd is arguing against Kepler's idea that astrological "aspects" or angles between planets cause these events</note>

<summary>Fludd argues that the Sun is merely a physical instrument or "mirror" for a higher, invisible Divine Wisdom (the Soul of the World). He contends that planetary harmony and earthly phenomena like earthquakes are caused by this divine Spirit rather than by Kepler’s geometric planetary angles.</summary>

<keywords>Invisible Sun, Wisdom, Sophia, Anima Mundi, World Soul, Metattron, Tetragrammaton, Johannes Kepler, Solar Body, Harmony of the World, Earth's Soul, Astrology vs. Divinity</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s argument against a purely mechanical or "pagan" view of the universe. He transitions from discussing the physical Sun to the role of Divine Wisdom (Sapientia) as the true mover of all natural phenomena, directly challenging the Aristotelian and Kepplerian reliance on secondary causes.</meta>

# ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE

...of the Planets, or excited by the soul of the earth. We find also that God renews the face of the earth (Psalm 104:30), not the Sun or the motion of the heavens, although this is commonly said. We are likewise taught in Leviticus 25:21 that God is the cause and author of fertility and the earth's produce; and that sterility of the earth happens because God shuts the heavens (Deuteronomy 11:17). But perhaps he <note>Likely referring to Johannes Kepler or a hypothetical Aristotelian opponent</note> will respond with that old axiom, that God acts in the highest heavens through the **ordered execution of the stars**. And indeed, I might have thought this very thing myself, had I not understood that the **throne of Jehovah** is in the heavens, but his footstool is upon the earth; unless, I say, I had perceived from the Holy Scriptures that the **Spirit of Wisdom**, by its own lucid act, fills all things and is present everywhere in the circuit of the earth (Wisdom 1). Indeed, of this Wisdom there is no number (Psalm 147:5).

I would have thought that all events of the lower world depended on the motions of the stars, and that storms and meteors <term>meteora; in this period, "meteors" referred to any atmospheric phenomena, including rain, wind, and lightning</term> were driven by them, had I not known from the testimony of Job 28 that this **Wisdom** is hidden from the eyes of all living things—the way to which God alone knows. It is He who gave weight to the winds, weighed the waters in a measure, set a law for the rains, and a path for the sounding storms. Had I not recognized that this Wisdom, present everywhere, is far more powerful than the Sun, the Moon, and all the stars; for by her power **Jehovah** founded the earth and established the heavens (Psalm 14). Her essence is as a breath of the power of God, a pure emanation of His glory; an act, finally, whose image is far more beautiful than the Sun and above every arrangement of the stars (Wisdom 7). 

I would have doubted, had I not known her to be older than every creature, as she was ordained from eternity before the earth was made. She set the decree for the sea, established the foundations of the earth, strengthened the fountains, fitted the heavens and made them firm, and set a compass upon the face of the abyss. I would have doubted, had I not perceived from the sacred writings that God, through this Wisdom of His, sent out snow like wool, scattered frost like ashes, cast down His ice like morsels, and by His word melted them again; and as soon as His wind blows, He makes the waters flow (Psalm 147). Unless I had believed, I say, that the thick clouds were the **chariot of God**, which stands upon the wings of the wind (Psalm 104:3), and that they received from His pavilion the light that illuminates everything in its circuit. By this, He does all that is prepared upon the surface of the world, whether for a scourge or for a blessing (Job 37:12). 

Unless I had found from many other places in the Holy Bible that the treasuries of **Jehovah** are the storehouse of snow, clouds, and thunders, I might perhaps believe either that the stars could produce all these things, or that the soul of the earth, stirred by anger, could perform such feats. But why should they search for such secrets of nature from the opinions of Aristotle or others? Or why should I attribute mysteries of such great importance to the earth, following the authority of the **Pagans** <note>original: "Ethnicorum"</note>? For we are taught in the third chapter of the Prophet Baruch that it is not fitting to desert the **fountain of all Wisdom** and seek art, science, and understanding in Pagan authors—as the Agarenes <note>Descendants of Hagar; here used to represent those outside the Judeo-Christian tradition</note> did, and as they seek in Theman—since they never recognized true Wisdom. 

To what end should I assign any faith to the Pagans, who place the wonderful operations of nature in the stars as active agents and the earth as a passive subject, saying they emanate from there, while they reject the Holy Scriptures, the fountain of all Wisdom? For unless Wisdom reveals herself to us, can anyone find the reason for the storms, the meteors, the winds, and the motions of the heavens? "Have you entered," says God, "into the treasures of the snow, or have you inspected the treasures of the hail?" (Job 38:22). "Who disposed the measures of the earth, since you are so knowledgeable? Or who stretched the line upon it? Upon what are its bases fastened, or who laid its cornerstone?" (Job 38:18). 

The works of God are indeed wonderful and vast, which He made in Wisdom (Psalm 104:24). Access to the knowledge of these is not granted except through that same Wisdom which made them. For to the **Theosophical** <term>Theosophico; someone who gains knowledge of the world through divine revelation rather than just human reason</term> man—not the worldly man—is given certain knowledge of the world's constitution: the virtues of the elements, the beginning, middle, and end of times, the mutations and Solstices, the varieties of seasons and storms, the circuit of the year, the positions of the stars, the natures of living creatures, the furies of wild beasts, the force of winds, the thoughts of men, the differences of plants, and the faculties of roots. In short, through her hidden power, the acquisitions of this world are revealed to him (Wisdom 7:17). 

This, therefore, is the true **star of the sacred fountain**, whose house stands upon seven columns <note>A reference to Proverbs 9:1, "Wisdom has built her house; she has hewn out its seven pillars"</note>; these are the seven true **Planets**, by whose observation and presence **true Astrology** is elicited, while the adulterous kind is relegated to eternal oblivion. This is the true **soul of worldly Harmony**, constantly striking the chords of concordant or discordant proportions according to the merit of the subject. It is she herself who turns the heavens and leads the Planets, inspiring them with a slow or swift soul according to her will. When she vacated her office, the Sun and Moon stood still in the middle of heaven and did not hasten to set for the space of a whole day (Joshua 10). By her retrograde motion, the Sun went backward when, in the time of Hezekiah, the shadow of the sun-dial was moved back ten degrees. 

What then do either the Pagans or their disciples know, to whom this Wisdom is unknown? Since, as the Royal Prophet witnesses (Psalm 92:7), "The brutish and foolish man does not understand the works of God." And (Psalm 88:13), "The works of God cannot be known in the dark." Therefore, the presence of the splendor of Wisdom is required so that we may be illuminated by it; without which, as Job witnesses (5:9), there is no searching out the great works of God, so wonderful and innumerable are they. Thus in Job 43:3 and Psalm 136:6, we find that the works of God are wonderful and beyond human grasp. And of the mysteries of the clouds it is said in Job 37:15: "Who knows the wonderful works of God, who is perfect in knowledge, concerning the balancing of the thick clouds?" 

It is **Divine Wisdom** to which we must recur for the knowledge of the true secrets of Nature, and not human wisdom, which is dull and infected with vain opinions. Therefore, there is need for the assistance of the **Divine Spirit**, who teaches all things (Psalm 143:7). This Spirit is Wisdom herself, who speaks of herself thus: "I, Wisdom, dwell with prudence, and find out knowledge of witty inventions" (Proverbs 8:12). This spirit is indeed the **Teacher of true art**, as we are taught in Exodus 28:3, 31:2, and 35:30. For the learned and wise of this world are fools before God (Matthew 11:25). He who, therefore, [seeks] true Wis...

He contrasts "Theosophical" knowledge derived from Scripture with the "vain opinions" of Aristotle and other non-Christian philosophers.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd's polemic against Johannes Kepler's *Harmonice Mundi* (The Harmony of the World). Having previously argued that Divine Wisdom is the true source of all motion, Fludd now transitions into a specific theological and philosophical attack on the Copernican (heliocentric) model adopted by Kepler, contrasting it with the traditional geocentric view supported by Scripture.</meta>

TO THE KEPLERIAN APPENDIX.
D 4

...they have acquired, she <note>Wisdom personified</note> herself pours out science and the knowledge of understanding like a rain, and exalts them with glory (*Ecclesiasticus 1:18*). God sends this spirit from the highest places to give understanding to men, so that they may know His secret counsels and works (*Wisdom of Solomon 9:17*). Whence, in *Proverbs 30:1*, Wisdom is called the "knowledge of the holy." And this is the reason why she is esteemed more precious than Carbuncles (*Proverbs 8:11* <note>original: "Prov. vb. 3." likely a misprint for 8:11 or 3:15</note>). Gold shall not be given for her, nor shall the price of her be weighed in silver, nor precious stones (*Job 28*).

Whence indeed, the greatest insanity of men is to speculate upon such secrets of nature and the admirable works of God without His Spirit—and much more, to boast that they understand them plainly and can demonstrate them by common Geometry. They claim to understand the harmony of His wonderful works, such as the positions of the stars and the ratios of their motions, so exactly that they do not fear to condemn the writings of other ancient Authors to whom the vision of truth appeared, all so that they may impose a more splendid face upon their own inventions. 

As for me, this suffices: that I know God is closer to the stars than we are, He who commands the stars, and whose spirit breathes where it wills without any hindrance from the stars or any evident obstacle from their radiations. I know, I say, that He Himself is closer to the earth than the stars, and His wisdom—which governs all things as He wills—is present to all. Whoever obtains this Wisdom is the true master of both types of Astronomy <note>Fludd refers to both the physical study of stars and the spiritual/divine "astronomy" of the soul</note>; without her presence, all Astrologers are blind. For such a man shall dominate the stars, since he is governed by a higher principle, which has made the stars and the elements themselves subject to him; he shall possess dominion over all things, like a true emperor of the heavens, until the last thing perishes. For, just as all things were made through Him, so also shall all things be transfigured through Him. This is that true Wisdom, which is, was, and shall be, and which reigns, and has reigned, and shall reign forever. We conclude, therefore, that this foundation of his <note>Kepler’s</note> "World Harmony" is a mere fiction, and in no way to be approved or esteemed by Christians.

Let us therefore arrive at the second species of his Music. In order to direct this toward the goal of his intention, he first makes the earth a star, moving around an immobile Sun. As Authors to support his side, he has Aristarchus of Samos the Pythagorean, Copernicus, and Tycho Brahe (concerning whom see Book 2, Proposition 25 and Book 5, Chapter 3). Against him, he has almost everyone experienced in Astrology and natural philosophy, except for those who, led by the imaginary opinion of the Magnet, imagine that the earth itself turns upon Magnetic poles <note>A reference to William Gilbert's *De Magnete* (1600), which influenced Kepler's theories on planetary motion</note>. The followers of Ptolemy are rejected, and they in turn reject these inventions and hold them as trifles. Hermes Trismegistus, Plato, and Aristotle placed the earth immobile at the center of the world, making it the place of rest. No one, either of the Ancients or the moderns, ever doubted whether the sun was mobile and the earth immobile. 

But all are deceived who, taught by "new experiment," affirm that the earth is a star and moves around an immobile sun between the orbits of Mars and Venus, as do Copernicus and Kepler. For, being informed by experience, we find that calculations according to the doctrine of Ptolemy have not wandered far from the goal or path of truth. Let us therefore approach the sacred Oracles <note>The Bible</note>, so that we may diligently inquire after their opinions in this cause. 

First, we ask: how is it possible that the immobile Sun is the source of motion, when it is the unique attribute of God that He moves all things while remaining immobile Himself? 

Second, how can the Sun be the source of motion (according to his own Chapter 9, Axiom 4 of Book 5) unless by the "Sun" he understands the very Wisdom of God, which alone is "more mobile and swifter than all mobile things" (*Wisdom 7*)?

Far be it from us Christians to ascribe an attribute of God to a creature. Furthermore, if the earth is a moving star in the heaven, there would be only one single heaven in which the body of the earth moves along with the bodies of the Planets. But if this is so, why did Moses say in *Deuteronomy 10:14*: "The heavens of heavens belong to the God of Gods"? And Nehemiah 1:7: "Jehovah made the heavens of heavens and all their host, the earth and whatever is in it," etc. 

Or, if the earth moves around the center of the Sun and has its motion from the Sun (the "source of motion"), what did that same earth do—having been created on the third day—before the placement of the Sun in the heaven <note>on the fourth day</note>? Did it rest? Or if it moved, from what source did it receive such motion, or around what fixed center? By the proportion of which regular body did it make its motion, or with which star did it resonate in harmonic consensus? 

Surely it is difficult to answer, insofar as the Sun and stars did not yet exist. But perhaps he will respond that the Sun was a congregation of rays of light dispersed through the vast cavity of heaven, and consequently, the earth could move by the virtue of that act. But we reply that it is necessary that such a motion would be outside the proportion of harmony, because it lacks a certain center around which to move, as well as other consonances to which it might relate by proportion. 

By similar reasoning we say that, if the harmony of the world depends on the diurnal motions of the Planets, it follows that before the placement of the stars in the heaven, there was no harmony in the world—which was completed on the third day. And yet the Holy Scriptures acknowledge a harmony which Wisdom (or the Word) placed in the world before the creation of the stars; for she "gave weight to the winds and weighed the waters in a measure" (*Job 28*). She "fitted and firmed the heavens, established the circuit upon the face of the deep, fortified the upper clouds from above, strengthened the fountains, and set the sea its decree." *Proverbs 22* <note>original: "vigesimosecundo," likely intended as Prov 8:27-29</note>. He "disposed the measures of the earth, and extended [the line] upon it; He stretched the North over the sea, and made the heaven revolve upon the Northern pole." *Job 38:4*.

<summary>Robert Fludd rejects Kepler’s heliocentric model, arguing that it ascribes the divine attribute of the "Unmoved Mover" to the Sun and contradicts the Biblical account of Creation where the Earth existed before the Sun.</summary>
<keywords>Robert Fludd, Johannes Kepler, Heliocentrism, Geocentrism, Divine Wisdom, Creation, Book of Job, Copernicus, Ptolemy, Biblical literalism, Harmony of the Spheres</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd's critique of Johannes Kepler. Here, Fludd defends the geocentric (Earth-centered) model of the universe against the heliocentric (Sun-centered) model proposed by Copernicus and Kepler, relying heavily on Biblical citations to argue that the Earth is stationary and the Sun is in motion. He dismisses Kepler's use of geometry and the five Platonic solids as mere "fiction" compared to the spiritual truth of the Scriptures.</meta>

# ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE

This is indeed the most exact Harmony of nature, which God administered in the creation of worldly matter and its frameworks. This is that modern harmony by which the parts of the world were so linked together in creation and are always preserved in the same consensus. But perhaps someone will say that circular motion was not in the heavens at that time, nor was there any harmony in it. But this is also completely false: For, as Wisdom says:
> *I alone encompassed the circuit of heaven <note>original: "ego rotunditatem cœlorum circumivi sola"</note> (that is, without the help or presence of the stars) and I walked in the depths of the abyss, in the waves of the sea, and in all the earth. (Ecclesiasticus 24:5)*

Thus, in its own harmony, this fiery love established the heavens at that time. Who will also doubt that Wisdom herself performed the cycles of the stars in the heavens and the earth before the stars were positioned in the sky? And does it seem probable that this power of the <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH, representing the divine source of all existence.</note>, this unique life of all things, should rest and be idle after the creation of the stars—she who is the true source of motion, more mobile than anything that moves? Indeed, this is that divine light which I have signified through my <term>formal pyramid</term> <note>A key concept in Fludd's philosophy representing the descent of divine light/spirit into matter.</note>, which was unequally present in worldly matter. Matter embraced this light more or less according to its greater or lesser dignity; the world is filled with its rays, which are invisible in themselves, and without which worldly matter would be reduced to nothing.

As for the mobility of the earth or the immobility of the Sun—since the Holy Scriptures seem to deny this entirely in the most express words—I see no reason why we should adhere to this opinion of *Copernicus* or his disciple *Kepler*, since the Scriptures are the source and origin of all truth. Regarding the nature of the earth in its mobility or firmness, we read in *Psalm 104:5*:
> *That Jehovah founded the earth upon its bases, so that it should not be moved forever or eternally.*

Similarly, concerning the property of the Sun, which they themselves called immobile, *Psalm 19:5* speaks in these words:
> *The Sun himself, like a bridegroom coming out of his chamber, rejoiced as a giant to run his course; his going forth is from the end of heaven, and his circuit even to the end of it.*

Similarly, in *Psalm 104:19*, it is said *that the Sun knows its setting*; and in *Psalm 113*, and concerning the rising of the Sun, etc., in *Psalm 104:22*. When God forbids the Sun, *it does not rise* (*Job 9:7* <note>The original cites Job 28:10, likely a mistake for 9:7.</note>). And *Joshua 10:13* handed down to us that *the Sun and Moon stood still in the middle of heaven for almost a whole day*. Likewise, we find that in the time of King *Hezekiah*, God made the *Sun go backward; indeed, the shadow of the sun-dial was drawn back by 10 degrees.* Furthermore, in *Ecclesiastes 1:4-6* <note>Original cites Ecclesiasticus 1:4, but the text is from Ecclesiastes.</note>, where both the earth and the Sun are discussed, we receive this discourse:
> *A generation passes away and a generation arrives; but the earth stands forever; the Sun rises and sets, and returns to its place, and there being reborn, it circles through the south and turns to the North; the spirit goes on surveying all things in its circuit, and returns to its cycles.*

What, therefore, can be clearer to prove that the earth rests and the Sun moves? What? Does *John* <note>Johannes Kepler</note> think that the Prophets were ignorant of the secret and mystical motions of the heavens, or that the calculation of the times and motions of the Planets was hidden from them, such that they did not know the difference between the mobility and immobility of the Sun and the earth? Surely we are taught in *1 Chronicles 12:32* that *from the tribe of Issachar there were those who had understanding of the times, to teach the Israelites what should be done; that is, so they might prescribe for them the hours and days of choice.* And *Esther 1:13: Therefore, the king speaking to the wise men who knew the times, etc.* From these things, one can see that if those skilled astronomers of that age had known such a mystery of motions, without a doubt such a great 
->error<-
would not have crept into the divine Bible.

Finally, as for the intervals having the proportion of the <term>five regular bodies</term> <note>The five Platonic solids: tetrahedron, cube, octahedron, dodecahedron, and icosahedron. Kepler famously used these to model the distances between planetary orbits.</note> by which the orbit of one Planet is distant from another, this ratio of dimensions seems very far removed from the true harmony of the world. Since the world had its complete harmony before the creation of the Planets (as declared above), we say that it is not a material proportion, but a formal one, which makes the physical dimension of the world's harmony. For it is indeed the divine spirit that moves and impels the Planets by various proportions of actions, both by natural and artificial motion—even if we have said that natural motion occurs more through the resistance and reaction of the body than by pure spiritual motion, which is why it is slower. That the spirit moves the Planet was said above from *Ecclesiastes 1*: *The spirit goes on surveying all things in its circuit, etc.*

But what more shall I say? The Philosophers themselves, whom he seems to cite for the invention of these bodies, in no way agree with his opinion, since they apply the use of these five bodies to the five elements of the world, whereas he applies them to the Planets and their distances. You see, therefore, how weak the foundations of the Author of the *Harmony of the World* <note>Kepler</note> are, and how remote from the testimony of the Bible. And yet he himself did not hesitate to break out into words of this kind in *Proposition 25, book 2*:
> *Nothing could seem more plausible than that the intervals of those six celestial orbits were taken from the five figures by the Creator in this order: so that the Cube is conceived in the mind between the orbits of Saturn and Jupiter, etc.*

He attributes this fiction of his, I say, to the Creator of the fabric, being ignorant of how difficult a thing it is to find such secrets of heaven without the help of the divine spirit. Hence it is said in *Job 38:37: Who shall declare the account of the heavens? etc.* For according to that in *Wisdom 9:15-17*:
> *Because of the weight of the soul in a troublesome body, we hardly guess those things that are on earth; but who shall search out those things that are in the heavens, and who shall know the counsel of God, unless God gives Wisdom and sends his holy spirit from the heavens?*

Hence, one can see that those Astronomical demonstrations and the unities of his Geometry—as well as his "harmony of the world" even down to the most harmonious intervals and numbers broken down into the smallest parts—are very unstable and published as opinion rather than confirmed by the truth of the matter.

Why should we make many words about the vain Astrology by which *Pico della Mirandola* <note>Giovanni Pico della Mirandola, a famous Renaissance philosopher who wrote a massive critique of astrology.</note>, in every kind of learning...

<summary>Fludd argues against Kepler's heliocentric model and his use of Platonic solids to explain planetary distances, asserting that Biblical authority proves the Earth is stationary. He claims true cosmic harmony is spiritual/formal rather than geometric/material.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Robert Fludd’s aggressive critique of Johannes Kepler’s mathematical approach to the universe. In the preceding pages, Fludd argued that true knowledge comes from divine revelation and the Holy Scriptures rather than the "fictions" of geometry. Here, he specifically targets the uncertainty of astrology and the vanity of trying to measure God’s creation using human tools like the ruler and compass.</meta>

# TO THE KEPPLERIAN APPENDIX

...so famous in times past, has he so weakened it? Tell me, why do you refer everything to the heavens, you Astrologer devoid of true wisdom? Why do you invent things concerning the natures of stars and signs, the motions of the Planets, and that most abstruse mystery of the <term>mundane Harmony</term> <note>Kepler’s theory that the movements of planets follow musical scales and geometric proportions.</note> arising from them, when you are in no way able to predict the powers and properties of even the smallest earthly thing? Why do you observe the stars and their motion—or rather, why do you lie about them—when you cannot even grasp a single tiny earthly body by measurement? What is more ridiculous, what is more absurd, than to try and happen upon the figures of the <term>eighth heaven</term> <note>The sphere of the fixed stars in the traditional geocentric model of the universe.</note> through the wandering connections of the stars? What extraordinary virtue do you believe such "imagined images" possess? What of the <term>triplicities</term> <note>Groups of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, Water).</note>, the <term>smoky, lucid, or empty degrees</term> <note>Traditional astrological classifications for specific points in the zodiac used to predict a person's fate or character.</note>, the Masculine and the Feminine? What harmony or stupendous events are brought about when they say the stars are affected by these things? What truth or virtue do these vanities of theirs display? Even if these motions, the natures of the stars, and the various adaptations of things among themselves seem to have some significance, I am nevertheless convinced that a man cannot properly discern them unless they are shown to him from heaven by a miracle, as is declared in the book of Wisdom.

<term>Porphyry</term> <note>A 3rd-century Neoplatonist philosopher.</note> said in vain that if someone could know the figure of their birth and the "Lord of the figure," they would find their own <term>Daemon</term> <note>A personal guiding spirit or genius, not necessarily an evil entity in this context.</note> or genius and be freed from the fate of their birth by it. But, he adds, it is impossible to know that figure or find this spirit because the rules of Astronomy and Astrology are incomprehensible and uncertain. But <term>Iamblichus</term> <note>A student of Porphyry who focused on theurgy and divine intervention.</note> says that the Spirit is neither known nor given by that birth chart and its "daemonic" influence—otherwise it would not free us from the fate of birth—but is given by a more ancient and higher principle, that is, by the spirit of the Gods. He asserts, following Porphyry’s opinion, that innumerable rules of astrology and doubts about the "genesis" (birth chart) are tossed around. From this it appears that knowledge of these things cannot be had. Therefore, he believes that the most certain truth regarding the stars can only be obtained through divine prophecy—that is, what is given to men by the spirit of wisdom. Once that is possessed, we have no need at all for the counting of rules and artificial predictions. Moreover, he adds that it is impossible for a man to demonstrate knowledge of this through mathematical science, since various opinions are held among us, some contradicting others. For in every science and art, because of dissenting opinions, we would be forced to say that no science exists at all. I have dealt with these matters abundantly in the 13th book of my Treatise on Nativities.

Hence the vanity of the mathematical sciences appears because of their corruption. Saint <term>Jerome</term> <note>A 4th-century Church Father known for his translation of the Bible into Latin (the Vulgate).</note> also mocks astrologers and those who cast birth charts in this way:

> These are they who lift themselves up against the knowledge of God, promising themselves a manufactured science of everything done in the world, which they refer to the rising and setting of the stars. These are they who are commonly called "Mathematicians," who think human affairs are ruled by the course of the stars and the gliding of the heavens; and while they were promising safety to others, they were ignorant of their own punishments. <note>original: "Hi sunt qui elevant se adversus scientiam Dei... sua ignorabant supplicia."</note>

Thus says Jerome. But in a word, I pray, let us attend to the speech of the Prophet Daniel teaching how frivolous it is to practice astrology in the absence of wisdom (Chapter 2, verse 27):

> *The mystery* which the king asks, the Wise men, the Magi, the Soothsayers, and the Diviners are unable to show to the king; but there is a God in heaven revealing mysteries, who will show it to you, etc. <note>original: "Mysterium, quod rex interrogat... revelans mysteria, qui indicabit tibi, &c."</note>

Even the ethnic Egyptians themselves confessed this, according to Genesis 41:8:

> *They called for all the Magi of Egypt and all its Wise men* etc. <note>original: "Accersiverunt omnes Magos AEgypti, & omnes Sapientes ejus"</note>

And there was no one who could interpret the dream for Pharaoh. Then it follows:

> *Do not interpretations belong to God?* (Genesis 40:8) <note>original: "Annon Dei sunt interpretationes?"</note>

> *Can we find a man like this* (meaning Joseph) *in whom the Spirit of the Gods is?* (Genesis 41:38) <note>original: "An invenire possumus huic... similem virum, in quo Spiritus Deorum sit."</note>

Since the Gods have made all things known to you, there is no one as prudent and wise as you (Genesis 41:39). And in Exodus 8, when Moses excelled the Magi in the production of lice, the Magi themselves confessed with these words:

> *This is the finger of God.* <note>original: "Digitus Deus est hic."</note>

Hence the Wise Man says in the passage cited above:

> *Who will investigate the things that are in heaven, and who shall know the counsels of God? Unless God has given wisdom and sent his spirit from the highest places.* <note>original: "Quæ sunt in cœlo quis investigabit... miserit spiritum suum e locis altissimis"</note>

Who also, by his own nature, is so wise in mind that he could find, by Geometric dimensions and the identity of lines, the reason how the earth is suspended over nothing? Or find the dimension of the cornerstone which the Creator threw down at the foundation of the earth? Or did God use "quadri-dimension" when he made a weight for the winds, weighed the waters in a measure, set a law for the rains, and a path for the sounding storms? Truly, Wisdom understands these things (Job 28:25).

> *Have you,* says God, *considered even the breadths of the earth? Tell me, if you know it all.* (Job 38:18) <note>original: "Nunquid, inquit Deus, considerasti usque ad latitudines terræ? Indica, si nosti totam illam"</note>

Does <term>Johannes</term> <note>Johannes Kepler.</note> really think that a harmony of this kind was made by the Creator according to the rule of <term>regular bodies</term> <note>The five Platonic solids (tetrahedron, cube, octahedron, dodecahedron, and icosahedron) which Kepler famously used to explain the orbits of the planets in his work *Mysterium Cosmographicum*.</note>, or cast together by his "quantitative lines"? Not in the least; rather, it was by an invisible power most full of divinity. For it is certain that the mundane harmony—indeed, the entire universe—is sustained by the wings of Wisdom, without whose divine Geometry not even a single moment could remain in its state. Let <term>Johannes</term> not boast of himself, therefore, or of his Astronomical Harmony, nor let him think that any certain rules exist in Astrology or Astronomy that are not contaminated by the corruption of human opinions. We conclude, therefore, that where the foundations are weak, cast upon a land of fables and drifting from the truth, it is impossible

-> for the entire building not to be <-
-> scattered by the wind and rain. <-

<summary>Fludd argues that Johannes Kepler’s mathematical and astronomical theories are "fables" compared to the certainty of divine revelation. He cites Biblical figures and ancient philosophers to prove that human measurement cannot grasp the mysteries of God’s creation or the true "Harmony of the World."</summary>
