<meta>Continuing from the previous page, Robert Fludd shifts from a direct attack on Kepler’s mathematical methods to a defense of his own cosmological system. He aims to prove that his "Worldly Harmony"—based on the interaction of light and darkness—is supported by both ancient mystical philosophy (Hermeticism) and Holy Scripture.</meta>

ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE

## *Philosophical and Theological Testimonies establishing our Worldly Harmony.*

First, I have supposed two contraries for the construction of my <term>Macrocosm</term> <note>The 'Great World' or the universe as a whole, which Fludd believed mirrored the 'Microcosm' of the human body.</note>, namely Darkness and Light, and that shapeless darkness existed within the divine power before the construction of the world. But truly, since the office of darkness was to deform and obscure, just as it is the office of light to form and illuminate, I have therefore said that these two principles are contrary by nature; insofar as the potential companion of Darkness (or shapeless matter) was cold, weight, and rest; while the companion and effect of Light was Heat, motion, and lightness. For in this way, even the divine power is said to be contrary to its own act, because it is hidden, invisible, at rest, and—as far as we are concerned—is nothing, insofar as it deforms everything by its absence. Yet the divine act differs in every way from its power regarding us, insofar as through it, and in it, God is revealed to the world, making all things visible out of nothing: For His presence informs all things. Hence, just as God, by His divine power, contracted His rays into Himself for the concealment of His Majesty—whereby all things were shapeless and overwhelmed by darkness—so indeed for His revelation He dilated Himself, and the darkness was driven from His lucid presence toward the extreme boundary of the center; that is, from the highest peak of the heavens downward to their lowest center. Therefore, the roots or bases of both my physical <term>pyramids</term> <note>Fludd used a famous diagram of two intersecting pyramids—one of light and one of darkness—to explain how spirit and matter interact in the universe.</note> are through God, from God, and toward God; namely, the dark matter from the divine power, which once purified ascends to God, and because it tends more toward its source, it participates step by step more and more in the light. For the confirmation of this our opinion, we have not only the account of the Philosophers of the highest divinity, but also the testimonies of the holy scriptures themselves. For <term>Hermes Trismegistus</term> <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure associated with a complex of philosophical and religious writings known as the Hermetica.</note> says in the *Pimander*, chapter 3: *An infinite shadow was in the abyss, and water and a thin spirit were in <term>Chaos</term> <note>The formless state of the universe before the creation of the cosmos.</note>: but a holy splendor flourished, which led out the elements from under the sand and moist nature; and when they were indistinct, the light ones soon flew up into the high region, while the heavy ones settled under the moist sand.* Here he seems to recognize that water and a thin spirit were potentially within the darkness, and that the heavy things submerged in darkness (that is, the Earth) settled under the moist sand (that is, in the Center) by the appearance of the divine splendor. Furthermore, he himself says in the first sermon of the *Pimander*: *The Divine Mind, by its power in the beginning, changed its form, and suddenly revealed the universe;* that is, from the mind of divine power was made the mind in divine act; that is, from the hidden God was made the manifest God, and He migrated from the <term>dark Aleph</term> into the <term>lucid Aleph</term> <note>original: "de Aleph tenebroso in Aleph lucidum." Fludd uses the first letter of the Hebrew alphabet, Aleph, to represent the primordial unity of God, transitioning from a state of hidden potential to revealed light.</note>. From which follows: *For I beheld a certain immense spectacle, namely all things converted into a light exceedingly sweet and pleasant, which wonderfully delighted me as I gazed upon it.* (This therefore is the true symbol of the <term>Tetrahedron</term> <note>A four-faced pyramid shape representing fire and the first stage of material light.</note>, or of fire and universal light according to the opinion of the Pythagoreans and Platonists, issued out of the Cube or Chaos.) Then he proceeds thus: *A little after, a certain horrendous shadow slid beneath in an oblique revolution, and migrated into a moist nature, then agitated by an ineffable countenance.* Here, then, the water is separated from the deformity of the darkness, and the second matter of all things comes forth into the light: whence he said in the fifth *Pimander*: *Shall I sing your praises in those things which you have brought out into the light from the darkness, or in those which still lie hidden, reclined in your secret bosom?* That is, in the water now brought into act, or that which is still hidden in the divine power and the deformed womb of <term>Hyle</term> <note>A Greek term for "matter" or "stuff," often used in philosophy to describe the raw, unformed substance of the universe.</note>. Then he descends, and thus proceeds from the two roots—namely light and water having been created—to the square or four elements, saying: *The True Word standing by the moist nature (here he denotes the union of the Word with the waters) cherished it; and from the bowels of the moist nature, a pure and light fire immediately flying out, sought the heights (and Genesis 1: The Spirit of the Lord was moved over the waters:)* *The light air also, obeying the spirit, took the middle region between fire and water:* *(concerning which, and through which after its purification and information, as St. Peter testifies in the 2nd Epistle 3:5-6: The heavens were of old, and the earth, consisting by the Word of God)* or from its bowels a pure and light fire leaped out; then afterward the air appeared in the middle place between the thick or sublunary water and the fire; but the earth and water were so mixed together that the thick water was with the subtle, or the dregs with the purer substance. We see, therefore, that here we have three things for a root; namely, *the power of God*, which is darkness; *the act of God*, which is light; and *the waters reduced into act*, or power reduced into act, or created waters, or waters mixed with light; which mixture was created from duality, consisting in uncreated unity. And in these consists that primary harmony of the world, according to whose exemplar the world was afterwards completed; whose proportion is as 1 to 2, and 2 to 3, and 3 to 4. For 1 reflecting into 1 represents the <term>quaternary number</term> <note>The number four, which Fludd links to the four elements and the four-letter name of God.</note>; in which number the letters of the <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>The sacred four-letter name of God in Hebrew, YHWH.</note> are found most exactly: but the remaining harmonies, which concur according to the exemplar of these proportions for the Harmonic disposition of the world, are composed of these, if we consider the matter exactly; such as the <term>Diadiapason</term> <note>A double octave (ratio 4:1).</note>, the <term>Diapente with Diapason</term> <note>A twelfth (ratio 3:1).</note>, the <term>Diapason with Diatessaron</term> <note>An eleventh (ratio 8:3).</note>, etc. Concerning which harmonies of the divine concordances we have clearly argued in our little book on the *Divine Numbers*. However, sufficiently valid testimonies from many places of the holy scriptures for these things of our structure...

<summary>Fludd explains the origin of the universe as a transition from God's "dark" potentiality to His "lucid" act of creation, using Hermetic texts and Genesis to justify his theory of elemental light and water. He establishes that the harmony of the world is based on numerical and musical proportions derived from the divine unity and the sacred name of God.</summary>

...the foundations or bases of our <term>Worldly Harmony</term> <note>Fludd’s theory that the proportions of the universe mirror musical intervals.</note> must be established: For in the first place, to confirm the <term>First Potential Matter</term> <note>original: *Materiam primam potentialem*; the raw, unformed substance of the universe before God acted upon it.</note> of the world—or the shapeless darkness, or those horrendous and lethal shadows out of which the Creator revealed and brought into the light the foundations of the world’s waters by the power of the shining Word—we find in Genesis 1: *that the earth was void, empty, or desolate*; that is, no light was actually present in the darkness to give it form, but it was destitute of every actual presence of God. But on the contrary, after this earth was created on the third day and filled by the act of God, it is said in the same place in verse 10: *And He saw that it was good*; that is, it participated in the divine goodness and act, without which nothing is good, and all things are called empty and desolate. Hence, the fertility of this created (that is, activated) earth followed in the sprouting of green herbs and the production of seeds, by reason of the divine power which it now possessed.

Furthermore, in the beginning of the same chapter it follows: *And darkness was over the face of the deep*; that is, all the potential waters, which he calls the "deep" (which were the material subject of the world), remained at rest in the presence of darkness; they stayed in that same potential state before they were brought into act by the manifestation or revelation of the shining Spirit of God <note>original: *Spiritus Elohim*</note>, becoming "something" out of "nothing." Whence it follows: *And the Spirit of the Lord moved over the waters*: And then the Lord said, *Let there be light*, that is, let the waters be tinted and informed by divine light. This water, thus tinted by the spirit, was afterwards called "created light." For this is the mixture of light with created water (or the creature), to which the form gave the name and the "Being" of light, or the <term>Empyrean Spirit</term> <note>The highest, most "fiery" and spiritual level of the heavens.</note>. But light in its own nature, without regard to matter, is purely a fiery Spirit, the divine offspring of God, or the first and immediate emanation from the very source of light.

And hence <term>Hermes Trismegistus</term> <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure associated with the origins of alchemy and philosophy.</note> said in the place cited above: *The Word stood by the moist nature and cherished it, etc.* And in the third Sermon: *The holy splendor flourished, etc.* And Saint Peter in his 2nd Epistle 3:5-6: *The heavens and the earth were consisting of water and through water by the Word of God*; that is, the water from which all things were made was informed by the presence of the divine Spirit, or the Word of God, or Wisdom. And just as form is mixed into any creature so that it may exist, so also divine light is within every structure, lest a vacuum exist in it; for without it, not a moment of time could subsist. This, I say, was that light—that distinguished and supreme Wisdom—which, according to the Wise Man in Proverbs 8: *Jehovah possessed in the beginning of his way, before his works; before any time, before the age she was anointed; before the beginning, before the first origins of the earth, when there were no depths, when he prepared the heavens, she was there; when he set a compass upon the face of the deep, when he established the clouds above, when he strengthened the fountains of the deep, when he gave the sea its decree, when he appointed the foundations of the earth, this light was with the Father like a foster-child, or the one composing all things.* This was she who established the heavens, gave weight to the winds, and weighed the waters by measure (Job 28). She disposed the measures of the earth, stretched the line upon it, and laid its cornerstone (Job 38:4). She stretched out the North over the empty space and hung the earth upon nothing; she surrounded the waters with a boundary, until light and darkness come to an end (Job 26).

Who can recount the wonders of this uncreated light? Who can express its power with the tongue—that light through which all things were made, and without which nothing was made; for in it is the life of all things? And this is that unique Word, the *Fiat* ("Let it be done"), hidden in the darkness before the creation of the world. For although it flowed out from the Father, it nevertheless returned into the Father, according to that saying of Hermes Trismegistus: *The Monad generates the Monad, and reflected its own heat into itself.* But when He decided by His secret counsel to fabricate the world in the womb of <term>Hyle</term> <note>original: *Hyle*; the Greek term for primordial matter.</note>, God revealed His form, and the Word now made came forth; by its power the waters appeared and became visible, and were distinguished into various proportions.

But if these things are not enough for those reading—since I know many have said that I have fallen into a profound error by asserting that the first matter was "unformed" (that is, uncreated)—I shall bring forth even clearer testimonies confirming my opinion to the mind; namely, proving that the foundations of the world’s waters leaped out from the belly of Matter and Chaos by the power of divine light. For first, in Job 12:22, we find this: *He reveals deep things out of darkness, and brings out into light the lethal shadow.* Here, by "revelation" he understands creation; by "deep things" he understands the waters or the informed abyss; and by "lethal shadow" he understands the earth which was empty, desolate, and void—which is called "lethal" because its soul was empty, but it has now been made alive. Thus, we perceive from this passage that heaven and earth themselves came out from the primary darkness, or the womb of Matter. Again we read thus in Wisdom 11:18: *God made the world out of formless matter*, see according to Pagninus’s translation; or according to Tremellius, *He created it*; or according to Jerome’s interpretation, *out of invisible matter.*

Here indeed the whole mystery of the entire creation is openly declared: Namely, first, that the first matter from which the world was created was uncreated or "uninformed" matter; which, insofar as it is shapeless, is called "nothing," because it is not "in act." And consequently, that darkness was as it were a mother, from whose belly the world (that is, the universal water) came forth. We have abundantly declared how God conducted Himself in the darkness. So also in 1 Chronicles 6:1 and 1 Kings 8:12, it is told that *Jehovah said he would dwell in the thick darkness.* And in Psalm 18:11: *He made darkness his secret place, and placed his tabernacle in the dark waters, and veiled it with thick clouds.* Similarly, Psalm 97:2: *Darkness and clouds are round about him.* And Psalm 104: *Thick clouds are the chariot of God.* And Psalm 105:39: *God spread a cloud for a covering.* And Job 22:15: *Thick clouds are the hiding place of God.* And although these things might not seem in all these places to be taken for the primordial darkness that was over the face of the deep; nevertheless, he who understands me rightly will realize that the reason for God's concealment in the primordial darkness is the same as the re-

<meta>catchword: velatio- (from "revelatio" or "revelation")</meta>

<summary>In this passage, Fludd argues that the universe was created by Divine Light informing a primordial "potential" darkness or "Hyle." He cites various biblical passages (Genesis, Job, Psalms) and Hermetic texts to prove that matter existed in a shapeless, dark state before God’s Word brought it into "act" and visibility.</summary>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page, Robert Fludd is establishing the "two foundations" of his cosmology. Having argued that the universe began in a state of potential darkness, he now describes how the Divine Light interacts with this darkness to create the physical world, using his signature imagery of two intersecting pyramids—one of light descending from God, and one of matter ascending from the center of the earth.</meta>

# ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE.

...revelation from these same things by the power of his fiery spirit, which was in the world after creation: Furthermore, according to that saying of *Hermes Trismegistus* <note>original: *Mercurij Trismegisti*; the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom various mystical and alchemical texts were attributed.</note> cited above, *Suddenly he changed his form, and all things were turned into an exceedingly pleasant light; and, a little later, a horrendous shadow by an oblique revolution glided underneath into a moist nature, then agitated with an ineffable countenance, etc.* At the presence of the Lord’s splendor, the abysses feared and were troubled. *Psalm 77:17.* For this same thing is also true of the primordial Darknesses, which were disturbed by the presence of the Lord and pushed downward. We have, therefore, now the two foundations and roots of nature, extracted from the darkness by the consensus of Philosophers and Prophets, which indeed stand as the bases of my pyramids by reason of opposition, because of the diversity of the natures themselves placed in the world. For what shall we consider more contrary than being and non-being, the Act and potential of God, his revelation and his concealment, light and darkness, day and night, motion and rest, heat and cold, etc.? For I think it escapes no wise man that all these contrarieties arise from contrary roots. 

But the medium of these is the watery nature, or moist nature, or **Pan**, <note>In Fludd's philosophy, Pan represents the totality of matter or the "all-encompassing" substance of nature.</note> which, insofar as it is the offspring of Chaos or darkness, suffers the acts of light or **Demogorgon**; <note>A name often used in Renaissance occultism to describe a primordial, formative deity or force of nature.</note> who is accustomed to procreate variations in the Elements and the heavens themselves. And now from the principle of primordial Harmony, which consists in the proportions of the roots, we shall descend to the middle Harmony, which acts symphonically in the square, or in the elementary middle: Which elements, although commonly speaking they are taken for the four sublunary regions <note>The physical world below the moon, consisting of Earth, Water, Air, and Fire.</note>, nevertheless in the truth of the matter they are found everywhere through the expansion of the entire world; namely, in whose substances light either more or less abounds; as was clearly declared in Book 1 of the *Physical History of the Macrocosm* chapter 9; namely in the Empyrean heaven, in angels, in stars both of the Zodiac and the wandering ones, and in lower things—indeed, in all things both simple and composite; but in a different manner in simplicity or purity, or grossness and impurity, which happens because of the abundance or lack of the presence and act of the secret light. 

Now, that the basis of the world's formal pyramid is continuous with God is declared from this: because God is the source of act, the father of all forms and light. For the creator created Wisdom before the age, and *she shall by no means fail for the age; in the holy tabernacle she ministered before him, Ecclesiasticus 24:10.* She is the vapor of the power of the strength of God, and a certain pure flux or emanation of the brightness of the Almighty God; She is the brightness and splendor of eternal light, and a mirror without stain of the divine Majesty, and the image of his goodness. *Wisdom 7.* Jehovah possessed her at the beginning of his way, and she is in the world composing all things. *Proverbs 8.* By her the heavens were formed, and by the Spirit from his mouth all their power, *Psalm 33.* This is that Word, which proceeds from the mouth of Jehovah. *Proverbs 2:6.* by which *all things in the world were made and continued, and without which nothing, since life is in it, John 1.* This is she who is drawn out of hidden things, that is, from the darkness, which is the booth of God; he himself understands its way and place. *Job 28.* 

Furthermore, that the flux of this Wisdom is permanent with God, from whom she emanates as if from a source, and is nonetheless continuous with him and indivisible, is indicated by *Job 11:6.* Hence, since it is certain that this emanation of form is a flux from a luminous source, or the supersubstantial kingdom itself, which the father fills with his presence—whose reflection in the world or luminous emanation is the first-born wisdom before any other creature, according to that *Psalm 84:12: God is a sun and a shield giving life and glory;* let us now seek where in the worldly fabric that basis of worldly light is found, which is in the world, and the world does not comprehend it; whether namely in the middle center of the semi-diameter, or the periphery. It is said in *Psalm 68:34: That God has ascended the heaven of ancient heavens;* And *God looked forth from the heavens upon the earth, Psalm 102:20.* And *who in the upper cloud is considered equal to Jehovah God? Psalm 89:9.* And *God dwells on high, who looks upon the lowest things in the heavens and on the earth. Psalm 113:5.* (here his "looking forth" is a splendid emanation.) Although Jehovah God is exalted, yet he looks upon the lowly. *Psalm 138:6.* 

Hence, therefore, it is said (to return to the matter) that God *sends forth his Wisdom from the holy heavens, from the throne of his glory he sends her. Wisdom 9:10.* And Wisdom opens her mouth in the assembly of the Most High, and glories before his power, saying: *I came out of the mouth of the Most High, and covered the earth like a mist. Ecclesiasticus 24:2. I had my tabernacle in high places, and my throne was a pillar of a cloud; I alone went around the circuit of heaven, and walked in the depth of the abysses, in the waves of the sea, and in all the earth. Ibid.* From which it is clear that his power and strength are everywhere. These things being considered, it is certain that Wisdom herself, which is the power or emanation of the <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH.</note>, tends from the circumference of the world downward toward the center; namely, from the luminous source situated at the summit of the heavens, it descends proportionally from the circumference of the machine toward the earth or center. 

But now, if we look back at the base in nature opposite to this, we will see that it occupies the very heart of the machine: For according to *Psalm 18:12: At the presence of his splendor the dark clouds vanished, the springs of waters became visible, and the foundations of the world were revealed.* Hence *Hermes Trismegistus*: *A horrendous shadow by an oblique revolution glided underneath at the sight of the divine light. Pimander 1.* From this it is clear that darkness flees from light: But, because the source of light was at the summit of the heavens, it is therefore probable that the darkness sought the center of the sphere, as a quiet place more remote from the center. And God stretched out the north over the empty and void (that is, he placed the heaven where the darkness or the void and desolate earth was) and hung the whole earth upon nothing, i.e., in the center or middle of the whole heaven without a support: That indeed, which is further declared by these words; *Who has numbered the heavens in Wisdom, and who makes those things which descend from the heavens to rest? When the dust descended into that hardness and the clods stuck together, Job 38:37.* What is clearer than this, that the black and dry dregs of the darkness or waters fell to the center of the machine and settled there? For according to that in *Genesis 1: The Earth was all that dry matter which, after the gathering of the visible waters below the heaven into their destined place, was seen, like a <term>caput mortuum</term> <note>Literally "dead head"; an alchemical term for the useless residue left behind after distillation or sublimation.</note> after distillati-*

<summary>Fludd defines the "two roots" of nature—light and darkness—as the bases of two opposing cosmic pyramids, identifying Wisdom (Sophia) as the divine emanation that descends from the heavens to give form to the world. He explains that while light originates at the periphery of the universe, the "dregs" of darkness and matter settle at the center, forming the earth.</summary>
<keywords>Robert Fludd, Hermes Trismegistus, Divine Wisdom, Sophia, cosmology, pyramids of light and matter, Pan, Demogorgon, Tetragrammaton, creation, emanation</keywords>

--- Page 57 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page, Fludd has just established that the "moist nature" or "Pan" is the medium between light and darkness. He now seeks to prove through ancient authorities and alchemical demonstrations that the universe is structured as two opposing, interpenetrating pyramids of light (form) and matter.</meta>

### TO THE KEPPLERIAN APPENDIX.

...the nature of dense and disturbed water. And this same point *Mercurius Trismegistus* <note>The legendary sage Hermes Trismegistus, supposed author of the Hermetic Corpus.</note> seems to confirm in this way:

> *Pimander 3.* The holy splendor flourished, which led the elements forth under the moist sand; and the heavy things, submerged in darkness, settled under the moist sand.

What, I ask, is clearer than this? From the testimonies of these passages, we conclude that just as the fount and basis of the lucid form—or formal light—is the throne of the Most High, exalted at the summit and peak of the heavens, so also its opposite is in the seat or place most remote from its actions. In the earth resides the gross or watery matter; this is that passive element upon which the pyramid of form, proportioned to it, acts. For as the form is, so also is the matter; because matter holds itself in the same proportion as the form itself, which causes it to be such as it exists. 

Wherefore we say that the assertion of *Johannes Kepler* <note>Johannes Kepler (1571–1630), the famed astronomer with whom Fludd engaged in a heated intellectual dispute regarding the use of mathematics versus mysticism in describing the cosmos.</note> is erroneous, in which he wished to posit in *Text 20* a formal pyramid unequal to the material proportion. For who, being consistent with himself, can doubt the figure of the physical formal pyramid if he follows these proportions of nature: the heavy, spongy Earth; Quicksilver <note>original: *Argentum vivum*; Mercury.</note>, metallic water, salt water, sweet insipid water, mist, clouds, vapor, air, exhalation, fire, the ethereal Spirit of many degrees, and finally the Empyrean Spirit—where, namely, the cone of the material pyramid ends, insofar as matter can be purified no further? 

The cone of this [material] pyramid, therefore, ends at the center of the formal basis. For if light was created on the first day, and this before the creation of the middle heaven, in what place would it exist if not in some third and most supreme heaven of all? If the light of the whole world was created within it, is it not likely that its spirit is in the ultimate degree of purity? For it is the nature of even the smallest light to act, and by acting to move, and by moving to heat, and by heating to thin <note>or "subtilize," making a substance less dense.</note> that which is thick. O, how pure and subtle must that spirit be which is the receptacle of such great, pleasant, and divine light! 

Do we not see by daily experience or "demonstration from the effect" <note>original: *demonstratione à posteriori*</note> that by the power of inferior fire there arises from wine a Spirit which is called the <term>quintessence</term> <gloss>the "fifth essence," a highly purified substance extracted from the four elements</gloss> because of its subtlety? Indeed, it is such because of the perfect heat of its internal light, and it claims the first place of dignity for itself by natural instinct. If the act of fire is applied to it, its power will imitate that act of divine light by burning—namely, without any injury or harm to flesh, or linen, or paper. <note>Fludd is describing the high-proof "spirit of wine" (ethanol) which can burn with a "cool" flame that does not immediately scorch materials soaked in it.</note> After this, the fiery Spirit of wine naturally ascends, which by the density of its substance burns and harms as it flames, because it has more matter and less fiery form; therefore, because of the strong resistance of the matter, it is accustomed to cause injury. Next succeeds the oil of tartar itself, which burns when compared to air, but in a more violent and rapid manner. To this succeeds the phlegm <note>The watery, dull distillate.</note>; then the liquor of dissolved Salt of Tartar; and finally the dregs <note>original: *fæx*; the sediment or waste</note> of the whole. 

Do we not see here the most exact order of all nature shining forth even in a tiny creature? And yet should we doubt the action and passion of these pyramids—namely, of light and the darkness of moist matter—when the formal substance of terrestrial fire is the same as that celestial fire from which it descended at the origin? Now also, which we think worthy of note, we see the heaven, the elements, and the earth in this single creature. Are not all the heavens found in proportion both in the earth itself and in the ethereal and empyrean heaven of the world? 

Indeed, if you have understood me rightly, God reigning in the highest summit of the heavens is nevertheless the center of every thing and part of the world, whose circumference is nonetheless outside all things. Take me, therefore, in either way in the higher sense, whether according to the common manner or the mystical; it matters not in this place. For a Bishop is said to be exalted in dignity, even though he does not depart from the earth in his station. For the Earth is accustomed to be converted into heaven by purification, and the imperfect is elevated into perfection without consideration of the measurement of lines; otherwise, clearly, than our *Johannes* [Kepler]—filled more with *Mathematical* doctrine than instructed in true Physical erudition—has supposed, who foolishly thinks all unities are to be measured by lines. 

Do we not see in man himself a heaven and an earth? And yet the earth is exterior, while the heaven is interior. Nevertheless, it is the summit of the heavens where the purest and simplest mind dwells; to which, nonetheless, the earth is called the center, insofar as the earth is further distant from the circumference or heaven. And thus the purity of God is as far distant as possible from the impurity of the dark earth.

But let us return to the matter. We see that any thing naturally tends toward its own country. For as a heavy thing tends downward and rests there, so fire tends upward, and in its motion, according to the condition of its nature, it makes a pyramid, as we see in a candle. This figure, indeed, consists more in smoky matter in its ascent than in the nature of pure light; and yet such is its form, because the disposition of the form made it so by natural instinct. What we understand by "physical pyramids" we have explained in a certain book destined for this thing alone, in the *Technical History of the Microcosm* <note>Fludd’s famous work *Utriusque Cosmi ... Historia*.</note>. Moreover, we received the ratios of these pyramids from the Wise Rabbis and elsewhere. For in the book *On the Gate of Light*, chapter 2 <note>original: *De porta lucis*; referring to the *Sha'are Orah* by Joseph Gikatilla, a fundamental text of Spanish Kabbalah translated into Latin.</note>, it is said that all things having life tend upward by their instinct—that is, toward perfection—and all things infusing life always verge downward. Hence, therefore, the appetite of matter tending upward toward the palace of form, and the office of inflowing form, is declared clearly enough. Furthermore, considering these in the dignity and vileness of the subject, *Reuchlin* <note>Johann Reuchlin (1455–1522), a German humanist and scholar who introduced Kabbalah to the Christian world.</note> describes the polishes of these pyramids thus:

> The sublimation of metals occurs when that which is more noble boils up into higher spirits, having been sublimated by Alchemy, so that those things which are higher always seem purer. So also among the inhabitants of the lower world, things are said to be livid and squalid, depressed by filth; while we marvel at those things among the inhabitants of the higher world, which are white and illustrious, adorned with incredible beauty, and we pursue them with proclamations of praise. 

Thus he spoke. And certainly, if one proceeds from the highest imperfection to more-than-perfection by the act of light, there indeed the degrees and proportions of harmony are the same, and—

<summary>Fludd argues that the universe is structured as two interpenetrating pyramids of light and matter, critiquing Kepler's mathematical approach in favor of a mystical, alchemical worldview where matter is purified into spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Robert Fludd, Johannes Kepler, Hermes Trismegistus, pyramids of light, alchemy, quintessence, spirit of wine, Johann Reuchlin, Joseph Gikatilla, Gate of Light, macrocosm, microcosm, formal and material pyramids</keywords>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page, Fludd remains engaged in his critique of Johannes Kepler. Having established that matter and form are two interlocking pyramids, he now argues that the same proportions found in the vast universe (Macrocosm) are perfectly mirrored in the smallest creature (Microcosm), and that these are governed by "physical" and "formal" harmonies rather than Kepler’s purely geometric ones.</meta>

### ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE

Now, in a tiny creature leaping forth from the disturbed abyss, we shall find the same things as in the Macrocosm <note>The "Macrocosm" refers to the entire universe; the "Microcosm" refers to the human being or any small part that reflects the whole.</note> itself; and the halfway point of motion from the squalid or imperfect base to that most exalted divine light continuous with the Deity, will be the seat or degree of perfection—the seat of the soul of the world, both in the small and the large, through which the sphere of equality passes. Here, a double proportion is made, or the string of the Monochord <note>A one-stringed instrument used to demonstrate mathematical relationships between musical intervals.</note> pressed in the middle sounds an Octave <note>original: "Diapason"</note> relative to the starting point (the base of the earth or imperfection) and to the goal of perfection which is contiguous with God. Thus, the proportion from the starting point to the middle—or the place of the Sun—relative to the whole string reaching from earth to God, is as 1 to 4, or as an Octave to a Double Octave <note>original: "Disdiapason"</note>, or as 1 to 2.

I would wish **Johannes Kepler** to understand, however, that in the measurement of this chord there is no need for linear measures or mathematical proportions, but purely physical and formal ones, without which it is impossible to progress in natural Music. The Alchemists <note>original: "Chymici"</note> indeed seem to hint at this, though in enigmatic speech, saying that everything naturally flees from its adversary but is delighted by its like. From this they conclude that the Sun—or Sulphur <note>In alchemy, Sulphur represents the active, fiery, and masculine principle.</note>—is nothing other than pure fire hidden in Mercury <note>original: "Mercurio"; the passive, watery, and feminine principle.</note>. They say that its weights and properties are relative to the sulphur existing within the Mercury. For the element of fire, which does not dominate in raw Mercury, is that very thing which digests matter, whether in the Macrocosm or the Microcosm and other creatures—indeed, even in a ripening apple. Therefore, everyone who concerns themselves with true Philosophy must know how much subtler the element of fire is than the other elements, and by what proportion it is able to overcome them. The weight, therefore, is in the first elementary composition of Mercury and not in any other work.

By a clearly similar reasoning, the composition of our natural pyramids is made in the great and lesser world, and the true harmony of both is adapted to such proportions. The Sun, therefore, receives its light through an Octave from God, through the Spirit of Wisdom, and it disperses the gifts of Wisdom through the worldly Spirit. And this same string of harmony exists in the world just as it does in a creature of a single inch. For the progression from the highest degree of imperfection to the highest goal of perfection through the moderately perfect is present in the smallest thing as much as the largest. In the most imperfect degree, there is the smallest portion of light, or fire, or sulphur; and so the portion of fire progresses by gradually overcoming matter, until it turns the whole matter into a fiery nature or a sun of the highest degree.

In the microcosm <note>The human being.</note>, there is a higher degree of Wisdom than in any other creature; and therefore, it can most easily be led back to divine perfection with the assistance of grace. Hence **Reuchlin** <note>Johann Reuchlin (1455–1522), a German humanist and scholar of Greek and Hebrew who introduced Kabbalistic thought to the Renaissance.</note> says: *“God composed man of a better nature, as the most sacred Scripture shows, from two things: the mud of the earth and the breath of life; so that clothed in mud he might prudently care for bodies, and endowed with the breath of life he might wisely love divine things and aspire to them.”* And **Mercurius Trismegistus** says: *“Man is a great miracle, an animal to be adored and honored; for he passes into the nature of God.”* And **Saint Paul** says: *“From corruption he shall ascend to incorruption; from a mortal he shall become immortal.”* <note>A paraphrase of 1 Corinthians 15:53.</note>

For this reason Adam, who was the root of the whole human race, being infected, infected all the branches and leaves of the tree, according to that passage in Romans <note>Actually Hebrews 12:15.</note>: *“The root of bitterness springing up hinders, and through it many are defiled.”* <note>original: "Radix amaritudinis sursum germinans impedit, & per illam inquinantur multi"</note> But a holy root produces holy branches; and such was the second Adam <note>Christ.</note>, who descended as a root and moves against the motions of the lower root. For the earthly Adam moves against the celestial Adam. These are the words of the **Archangelus** <note>Likely referring to Archangelus de Burgonovo, a Franciscan Kabbalist.</note> in his Kabbalistic doctrine. By these sayings, they seem to suggest that internal or formal motion, which we have made pyramidal, into the external motion of man himself.

But if one speaks of the descending act and the ascending passive part of the Macrocosm: whatever stands apart from God in the first and purest act must necessarily enter into matter. Whence the naturalists, describing the breadth of beings, place pure act without matter at its summit, and at the bottom is matter without form. So says he: By "matter without form," he understands the earth, empty in itself, and fertile by accident, insofar as it receives the emitted influences of God and the **Sephiroth** <note>In the Kabbalah, the ten attributes or emanations through which the Infinite (Ein Sof) reveals itself.</note> from above, and is their receptacle and, as it were, a matrix <note>A womb or nurturing environment.</note>. From its belly, a smoky matter ascending by degrees is always more dilated the further up it tends; and this is because of its increase in the proportion of the lucid acts, whose nature is always to tend toward purification and refinement.

Therefore, as matter holds itself in purity, so we are accustomed to judge the proportion of the form inherent in it. For we can see the proportion of the acting nature by the effect alone, and not otherwise; because it is the "invisible fire" of **Zoroaster** and **Heraclitus**, of which Job 28:12 says: *“Wisdom is drawn from hidden places; it is hidden from the eyes of all living things; it also lies hidden from the birds of the heaven.”* <note>original: "Trahitur Sapientia de occultis, abscondita est ab oculis omnium viventium: Volucres quoque cœli latet."</note>

We conclude, therefore, as was said before: that just as the soul is said to be in the whole and in every part of the whole, so also the pyramidal progression of physical matter proves that such an internal form exists within it as the matter itself is. And consequently, that mathematical Musician <note>Johannes Kepler.</note> spoke ill-advisedly when he said the formal pyramid was unequal to the material one. For such as the disposition of matter is, such also is the proportion of form; since it is the nature of form to make the matter such as it is itself.

But it is certain that matter in its ascent has a pyramidal progression understood in a physical way; insofar as water is ten times subtler than earth, and air than water, and fire than air, and the quintessence (or the matter of heaven) than fire; and so with the rest. Indeed, in any element there are infinite degrees of subtleness, which it is impossible to recount here. From this it is clear how futile is that argument of Johannes, by which he tries to prove that there is no unity in a natural thing that cannot be measured by those same lines—as if, forsooth, the same handful of earth could not be converted into water, where the units differ because of the dilation of one and the constriction of the other in the mathematical manner, and yet by physical reasoning they are the same when considering their philosophical weights. For a greater proportion of fire or sulphur proves a subtler substance, and a lesser proportion a grosser one; so that both in subtleness and grossness,

He dismisses Kepler's geometric requirements, asserting that the spiritual and physical worlds are harmonized through alchemical and Kabbalistic principles rather than simple linear measurement.</summary>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page, Fludd has just argued that natural music requires "physical and formal" proportions rather than the purely mathematical ones proposed by Kepler. He is now explaining how these physical proportions manifest in the elements and the structure of the world, using the "Cube" as a symbol for physical reality.</meta>

# TO THE KEPLERIAN APPENDIX
### E 2
<meta>catchword: vi-</meta>

Whether in perfection or imperfection, the proportions of identity consist in Physical things; these behave differently in Mathematical things. And yet, in the mathematical manner—that is, by mathematical pyramids and their units—I have demonstrated this, because it was impossible to aptly explain physical proportions in their own order; for in their motion they are infinite. From these pyramids mixed together, as if from the four universal elements, the **Cube** is composed in Physical reality, or let us say a composite body consisting of the number eight <note>The number eight, or the octonary, was associated with the cube (having 8 corners) and represented the material, physical world in Pythagorean thought.</note>, which by its own nature is earth or clay, or some similar compact thing, whether in the sublunary or the celestial heaven. And in this final species of worldly harmony, there is a double difference in the subject and its motion, by which it is carried either toward generation, or corruption, or augmentation, or diminution. (The effect of which is declared by constant experience in the composite things of the animal and vegetable kingdoms, as well as the mineral.) Or by which it always remains such as it was made in creation, but constantly receives life and motion from the act of **Divine Wisdom** walking everywhere in the cavity of the world: Life, I say, it receives, by which it exists and has continuation in the nature of things; and it obtains motion, whether manifest or hidden, so that it may perform that office for which it was created and destined by its Creator. Of this kind are mineral bodies in the sublunary regions, inasmuch as they live a hidden life and have this duty: to serve man. In the celestial regions, however, they are the bodies of the stars, whose duty is that, while the Sun dominates by day, the dominion of the day is maintained, and the shadows of night are transformed into the light of day; and while the Moon dominates with the other stars, light is given to the night veiled in attacking shadows, and so that they may become signs of the seasons, days, and years. Their motions are included in that **Harmony**, which emanates from the sympathy of the universal elements in no other way than these emanate from the triple, simple, and primordial Harmony. Therefore, the concert of the stars is contained in the **Cubic species of harmony**, which exists according to the greater or lesser predominance of light in the stellar bodies; by which, according to the said proportions, it moves the bodies in which it dwells either faster or slower, and places them higher or lower in the ether and its orbs.

For thus we see that a weapon of war <note>original: "tormentum bellicum," referring to a cannon.</note> loaded with more gunpowder usually carries its ball further and faster, and an artificial rocket <note>original: "fusum artificiale." Fludd uses the analogy of a projectile or firework to explain how the "fuel" of light or spirit drives planetary motion.</note>, according to a greater proportion of its fuel or flame, is carried higher toward the region of heaven. Therefore, these motions are durable, and the bodies in which they exist are also such by nature. But truly, since the motion of the stars proceeds from the elements of heaven themselves, it is clear that the harmony also proceeding from there is derived from the elements themselves; for in them the nature of things or **Wisdom** lay hidden before the stars were created. For it is necessary that the thing produced follows the principle, just as a son follows a father, and also the "elemented" follows the element. For as it is absurd to attribute the origin of a father's life to the son, so too it is no less absurd to apply the harmony of the elements to the Planets themselves, which are things made of elements. And if you wish to know from the testimony of the sacred font what this elementary harmony is like, and what the motion is from which it arises, read chapter 19 and the last of **Wisdom** <note>The Book of the Wisdom of Solomon, an apocryphal/deuterocanonical book.</note> from verse 17, where you will find words of this kind, brilliantly revealing the motion and Music of the elements:

> *For while the elements are changed among themselves, just as in a musical instrument <note>original: "organo."</note> the sound of the quality is changed on the psaltery (and all things keep their own sound; which can be estimated with certainty from the sight itself: For earthly things were turned into aquatic, and whatever were swimming things passed over to the land; fire prevailed in water beyond its own power, and water forgot its extinguishing nature, etc.)* <note>original: "In se enim dum elementa convertuntur... aqua extinguentis naturae obliviscebatur &c." Fludd is quoting the Vulgate version of Wisdom of Solomon 19:18-20, though his numbering differs slightly.</note>

Where indeed one may perceive the force and harmony of the elements; in which harmony, however, no mention at all is made of that of the Planets. Indeed, this kind of harmonic action and passion of the elements exercised its powers in the worldly pyramids long before the fourth day of creation; for there was a rotation of one element into another, through which motion occurred from the immaturity of plants toward perfect maturity by the motion and proportion of the increase of **Sulfur in Mercury** <note>In Alchemical theory, Sulfur represents the active, masculine, fiery principle, and Mercury represents the passive, feminine, watery principle. Their union creates all things.</note>, or of fire in water, as much in the thin as in the thick, and from a simple number to the multiplication of species through the created seedbeds. Whence, says God before the fourth day, addressing his Wisdom:

> *Let the earth bring forth green herbs, and those making seed according to their kind, and the fruit-bearing tree making fruit according to its kind, and each one having seed according to its species: And God saw that it was good.* <note>Genesis 1:11-12. Fludd emphasizes that life and harmony existed before the Sun and Moon were created on the fourth day.</note>

But if this harmony was infused into these composite creatures after the creation of the elements, how much more did God infuse a similar force of harmony into the elements themselves: Whence it was said after the appearance of the Divine Spirit:

> *And God saw the light, that it was good; And he made the firmament, which he called heaven: And God said, let the waters which are under the heaven be gathered together, and let the dry land appear, and he called the dry land earth, etc. Genesis 1.*

Is there not here an admirable harmony of elements in the **worldly organ**, whose establisher was **Elohim**, but the sound or voice was the **Word** from his mouth? And the adapter of the harmonic consonances and powers emanating from them was that fiery **Spirit** or the form of worldly harmony proceeding from the breather and the voice, infusing power and act into each element in its own degree, and according to the dignity of its matter. Which indeed is best declared by these words of the Royal Psalmist in Psalm 33:6: *By the word of the Lord the heavens were established, and all their power by the spirit from his mouth.* From this spirit of Wisdom, therefore, proceeds the Harmony of the whole world, attributing and conferring power to each species according to its proportion ordained by the Creator in creation for the legitimate execution of its office. Nor is this spirit to be sought in heaven in the vulgar way of understanding; for it is everywhere in the expanse of the world, and everywhere always works, according to that in Matthew: *Heaven is his throne, and the earth the footstool of his feet,* and Wisdom 1:6: *The Spirit of the Lord fills the orb of the earth, and that which contains all things has knowledge of the voice.* This, then, is the true **Sun of nature**,

<summary>Fludd argues that universal harmony originates in the four elements and the Divine Spirit rather than the planets, using alchemical principles and biblical citations to prove that life and order existed before the celestial bodies were formed.</summary>

He is transitioning into a detailed explanation of how the number three (the ternary) governs the spiritual and formal structure of the universe, contrasting it with the number two (the binary) which governs matter.</meta>

# ANALYTICAL DISCOURSE

...giving life and glory to the world and its creatures, and cherishing the heavenly hosts with his presence, and causing them to move; his hidden tabernacle is in the center of his elements, while his symphonic rays penetrate everywhere and are present everywhere. Now let us descend to the division of the world, or the four universal elements, into parts or the three heavens: For after the extension of the number two into its square <note>2 x 2 = 4</note> and its Cube <note>2 x 2 x 2 = 8</note>, there follows the number three, which includes that imperfect and material number along with the primordial unity. By this, two unities are thus fused into one number, which is the root of perfect things: So also its property in the world follows the watery nature divided into Elements. Therefore, according to the properties of the divine Trinity, the world is distinguished into three regions that do not differ in the matter of their physical units. Wisdom produced these distinctions; her spirit was the glue between water and light, or the offspring of divine power and its act: For that "act" is the effect of light, by which one unit of matter differs from another. And again, each of these heavens is subdivided into three other regions; just as the **Empyrean heaven** <note>The highest heaven, composed of pure light or fire, where God dwells.</note> is divided into three hierarchies, and each of these again into three other portions attributed to the orders of angels: and so with the rest. For this is the business and office of the fiery spirit, which distinguishes all things first through its root 3, then into its square 9 (precisely the number of regions into which each heaven is divided), and finally into the Cube 27, which according to the **Platonists** is the mathematical measure of the entire semi-diameter. Therefore, the triple division comes from the side of the soul or the power of the informing **Tetragrammaton** <note>original: "Tetragrammati"; the sacred four-letter name of God, YHVH.</note>, and the dual progression comes from the side of informed matter: For the universal elements were produced immediately after divine power was reduced into act; but the heavens were completed on the second and third day. For the multitude of heavens, we have testimony from the sacred scriptures: *Jehovah established the heavens by his wisdom* (Proverbs 3:19). *The heavens of heavens belong to the God of Gods* (Deuteronomy 10:14). *Jehovah made the heavens of heavens, and all their host* (Nehemiah 9:6). *The heavens of heavens, and the waters that are above the heavens, praise God* (Psalm 148:4). From these, it is clear that there is a number of heavens, which appears to be three; the first of these heavens was the work of the first day: For in what place would the light be put, if not in its own proper Empyrean heaven, since neither the ethereal nor the sublunary had been created yet? Does not Paul also say that he was caught up into the "third heaven," where he confesses to have seen wonders and ineffable things? Yet he <note>Referring back to Kepler's view, which Fludd is critiquing.</note> nonetheless insists that the entire harmony of the world arises from the extreme motions of the Planets: which motions are indeed circular, and consequently he denies that any harmony can be investigated in the degrees of distance between one end of the world-machine’s semi-diameter and the other, as appears from his text in chapters 18, 19, and 20. Nevertheless, we perceive that **Plato**, in his *Timaeus*, used the number three for the formation and measurement of the world; it is the first odd number and the root of the composite world by which it is distinguished into regions. Its square is 9 and its Cube is 27. By this number, he measured the ratio of the world's orbs, concluding that the further one proceeds from the periphery, the denser the degree of worldly substance becomes—no differently than progress is made from a root toward a Cube in the depth of a body. But truly, since three "nines" are found in 27, Plato arranged them in this way toward his Cube, by which he measured the world’s semi-diameter: 9, 9, 9. If **Aleph** <note>The first letter of the Hebrew alphabet, representing the number 1 and the Divine Unity.</note> is added to this, which signifies unity, the number 1,000 is produced <note>999 + 1 = 1000</note>, beyond which there is no further progression. Hence, just as the Hebrews have the *Small Aleph* <note>original: "Aleph parvulum"</note> for the unity and starting point of all things, so the *Great Aleph* <note>original: "Aleph magnum"</note> denotes the number one thousand, which is the goal. Hence the one God is called the **Alpha and Omega**, the beginning and the end: For the number ten is the root of the number one thousand (which is a Cube), and its square is 100. Thus, Plato most aptly delineated the fabric of the world in the number one thousand—namely 9, 9, 9—within which formal dimensions he placed the entire harmony of **Worldly Music** <note>original: "Musicae mundanae"</note>. **Francesco Giorgio of Venice** <note>original: "Franciscus Georges Venetianus"; a 16th-century Christian Kabbalist.</note> also seems to agree with this; he completed a whole, indeed a massive, volume on this same subject and wrote most learnedly. Therefore, the Hebrews establish that the first "nine" is a simple number, insofar as it is contained under 10; and they intend for it to be a formal and essential number because of its simplicity, attributing it to the nine orders of Angels who are in the intelligible world: the difference between these orders denotes the variety of degrees and harmony by which one excels another in proportion and disposition. **St. Dionysius**, **Isidore**, and other fathers of Theology fell in with this opinion. Is this harmony, then, from the motions of the Planets? No. The second order of tens, insofar as it is found in the square or the number 100, partakes of thicker matter; hence the 9 heavens are attributed to these. Finally, the third order descends into the Cubic number or 1,000, insofar as it is more material and closer to composition, in which are contained the 9 types of things that can be generated and corrupted in the elementary world. Plato and the other wise men recognized this descent from simplicity to composition, and in that descent, the harmonic propagation between one sphere and another. This metrical harmony of the world is not attributed to the motions of the Planets; for they make their own harmony, proper to themselves as particular creatures, and their duties pertain to sound in the world, and not to the universe as a whole. The universe's harmony consists in the greater or lesser proportion of eternal light in the world-spirit, or, *speaking chemically*, in the degree of **Sulfur** within **Mercury** <note>In Fludd's alchemy, Sulfur represents the active, solar light-principle, while Mercury represents the passive, fluid world-substance.</note>. Furthermore, the Author himself <note>Kepler</note> in the preface to his third book acknowledges that this descent from unity in the number nine was the opinion of **Pythagoras**, who in his **Tetractys** <note>A triangular figure of ten points, sacred to Pythagoreans, representing the organization of space and the universe.</note> makes the matter of the world descend in my fashion from unity into an even number from the root 2, through the square 4, to the

<meta>Catchword: Cu-</meta>

<summary>Fludd argues that the universe is structured by the number three, creating a hierarchy of nine-fold divisions across the angelic, celestial, and elemental realms. He rejects Kepler's focus on planetary motion, asserting that true cosmic harmony is found in the varying proportions of divine light within the world-spirit.</summary>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page, Fludd transitions from a general discussion of Divine Wisdom into a specific mathematical and harmonic demonstration of how the universe is structured. He is using the "Platonic Lambda"—a triangular arrangement of numbers—to show how the world descends from the unity of God into the complexity of material existence, responding to arguments potentially raised by the astronomer Johannes Kepler.</meta>

# TO THE APPENDIX OF KEPLER'S ANSWERS
### PAGE 53

The Cube 8, and even the form itself, shares the same harmony with matter, moving from unity into an odd <note>In Pythagorean thought, odd numbers were often associated with "form" or "spirit," while even numbers were associated with "matter."</note> number; and thus the harmonic progression of the world occurs from simplicity toward multiplicity in this manner:

<image-desc>A diagram illustrating the harmonic divisions of the World Soul, often called the Platonic Lambda. At the peak is the number 1. Below it, numbers are arranged in a triangular grid: 2 and 3; then 4, 6, and 9; then 8, 12, 18, and 27. Dotted lines connect these numbers to form nested triangles, with the numbers 2, 4, 8 descending on the left (powers of 2) and 3, 9, 27 on the right (powers of 3), while 6, 12, and 18 occupy the interior positions.</image-desc>

Here, starting from the number two, twice 2 are 4; twice 3 are 6; twice 4 are 8; twice 6 are 12; twice 9 are 18. Then we proceed to the number three, and we say that three times 2 are 6; three times 4 are 12; three times 6 are 18; and three times 9 are 27. 

From the first triangle, therefore—1, 2, 3—we conceive the first and simplest type of composition, which is made from the elements of the **Empyrean heaven** <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the universe, traditionally the dwelling place of God and angels.</note>, and it is that which consists in the radical number of the angels. From the second triangle—1, 4, 9—we gather the composition of the secondary type, namely from the **aethereal elements**; and this belongs to the stars, contained within the square number. However, in that largest triangle—1, 8, 27—the entire universe stands with its foundation; this denotes the composition from the **sublunary elements** <note>The "lower" world beneath the moon, consisting of Earth, Water, Air, and Fire.</note>, from which imperfect bodies (such as meteors) and perfectly composed bodies (such as animals, vegetables, and minerals) are formed. 

The reason for this is that the substance of the elements is ordered on the left side, while their form is ordered on the right. Here we see that 1 is the author of both the odd and even numbers, distributed equally to each side. For from 1, as if from an eternal power, matter is derived on one side, which is also brought into act from the 1. From this, 2 becomes 3, and they denote the bond and unity of light with spirit in the first composition of elements. 

Then, on the left side of the second triangle, 1 is joined with 4 (the square of matter), and the second composition of elements is made; finally, 1 looks down toward 8 and produces the coarser elements of the third composition. Therefore, in the highest region, unity is joined with 2 by an inviolable bond, and the angelic soul is created with such a body through the number 3, where 1 is the soul and 2 is the body. 

In the middle region, 3 mixes itself with 4 and 2, and the stellar soul and body are made according to the perfection of the number 9; here, 4 and 2 indicate 6 (the corporeal elements of a star), and 3 (by whose addition 9, the square of the number three, is produced) indicates its soul. From these things it is clear enough that the angelic composition of the higher heaven, which consists in 3, is the soul of the bodies of the middle heaven. 

<margin>The Cube 8 above the square...</margin> <margin>The Cabalists call the World Soul the Angel Metatron.</margin>

Hence the Cabalists <note>Followers of Jewish mysticism, whose ideas Fludd often integrated into his Christian cosmology.</note> called the **World Soul** <term>Angel Metatron</term> <note>In mystical tradition, Metatron is a high-ranking angel often associated with the celestial scribe or the link between the divine and the mundane.</note>. Finally, 4 and 8 make 12, by which number it is right to indicate an imperfectly mixed body; if the planetary body (namely 6) is added to this, it teaches the complete composition from the elements, namely 18, whose form will be 9 (the entire composition of the heaven), which is called the "life of the lower world," though understood in a hidden way. And so, the composition of the creature made of elements will be complete in 27, which is the end of both the world and the things created within it.

There is, therefore, a <term>superbipartient</term> <note>A technical term in music theory and arithmetic referring to a ratio where the larger number contains the smaller number plus two parts of it (e.g., 5:3 or 4:3).</note> proportion in the second elements, such as 2 to 1, 4 to 2, and 8 to 4. Similarly, one proceeds to the procreation of creatures in a <term>superbipartient third</term> proportion, such as 6 to 3 and 12 to 6. And for the union of the composed creature with its elements, the power of a triple nature is required, which is the "ligament" of the elements. In its simple formal progression, it proceeds from the root 3 to 27, and by reason of composition, as 18 to 9 and 27 to 18. 

For in this progression, the number nine always increases itself by its own dimension until it reaches the cube of its root. From these things, one can see that the soul of the Empyrean heaven and the form of the angels is an immediate emanation from 1, and the entire composition of this heaven is like the soul of the middle heaven, whose "fifth essence" <note>Also known as the *quintessence*, the pure substance of the stars.</note> is called the life of the lower things. And yet, both the matter (whose immersion is into the even multiplicity) and the form (which descends on the right side from unity to the odd multiplicity) have their origin in 1. Thus, the whole—both formal and material—is in truth nothing other than merely "One," although to us it seems to be immersed in multiplicity. 

On this, therefore, depends the **universal Harmony of the world**: from here all proportions arise to which the Music of creatures is adapted. For in the **double proportion** <note>The ratio 2:1, which creates the octave.</note> consist 2 to 1, 4 to 2, and 8 to 4, from which the <term>Diapason</term> <note>The Greek term for an octave.</note> and its doublings are formed; consequently, the creation of the elements from which the world is built arises from it. 

In the **sesquialtera** proportion <note>The ratio 3:2, which creates the musical interval of a fifth.</note> we find the relationship of elementary matter to the form of the composite; thus 3 relates to 2, 9 to 6, 6 to 4, 12 to 8, 18 to 12, and 27 to 18. From this follows the complete mixing of form with elementary matter. 

In the **sesquiquartia** proportion <note>The ratio 5:4, or in this context, a general term for ratios like 4:3 (usually called sesquitertia), though Fludd uses his own terminology here for the fourth.</note>, imperfectly mixed things are produced, sounding between matter and form to complete the composition: thus 4 relates to 3, 8 to 6, and 12 to 9. And so the Harmony of all nature is completed, initiated from a "supereffential point" <note>God, who is beyond all essence or being.</note>. 

From the inspection and observation of that figure, we perceive that the creatures of the Empyrean heaven (that is, of the highest created purity) are composed of two things: namely, light and spirit. From the observation of its base, those of the middle heaven are composed of three: namely, from the two higher things and a third, coarser spirit, as is clear from the base 4, 6, 9. Finally, that lowest region is composed of four: namely, from the sublunary elements increased in proportion. Thus we see that 2 proceeded immediately from 1 by doubling, from which, with 1 added, becomes 3, or the triangle of the first composition, denoting...

<summary>Robert Fludd uses the "Platonic Lambda" diagram to explain how the entire universe—from angels and stars down to minerals—is structured according to harmonic musical ratios (octaves, fifths, and fourths). He argues that while the world appears complex and material, it all mathematically originates from the divine Unity (1).</summary>

<meta>This page concludes Robert Fludd’s analytical discourse against Johannes Kepler. Fludd has been arguing that the harmony of the universe is based on a descending numerical progression (from the spiritual to the material) rather than on the physical speeds and motions of the planets, as Kepler proposed. This page summarizes his metaphysical geometry and announces his future works.</meta>

# ANALYTIC DISCOURSE
## PAGE 54

<margin>22869 54 Kepp</margin>...otherness <note>original: *alteritatem*; in Fludd's philosophy, this refers to the distinction between the Creator's unity and the "otherness" of created matter.</note>, or the angelic nature; according to whose pattern all the small parts of the world were established, according to that saying in Job: *Who laid its cornerstone?* <note>Job 38:6. Fludd views the universe as a building constructed on divine geometric principles.</note> Thus the triangle stands as in the higher place: 1, 2, 3. In this position, the first two letters of the Tetragrammaton <note>The sacred four-letter name of God. Here Fludd uses the first two: Yod (י) and He (ה).</note> are attributed to this heaven or composition. Then, by adding 3 to the number 4 in a transverse way, it produces the number seven, which is the attribute of Man’s life, just as the Hexad <note>The number six, often associated with the six days of creation and the physical structure of the body.</note> is attributed to his body. Hence it is said: *He made man a little lower than the angels* (Psalm 8). 

But in the order of nature, 1, 2, 4 on one side and 3, 9 on the other denote the Triangle of the second composition, which relates to the first as a square does to its root; therefore, the middle heaven denotes its bodies, which are defined as the denser parts of those orbs. From these things it is clear that both heavens are found abundantly in the internal man. The remaining creatures, however, are made from the descent and composition of the deeper soul into the matter of the earth. Whence the wise have adapted the Ennead <note>The number nine, representing the full manifestation of the formal world into matter.</note> to all creatures: for the triangle seeks a deeper base for their composition, and then it will stand thus: 1, 2, 4, 8 on one side, and 1, 3, 9, 27 on the other, as seen above. Thus the first triangle is like the root to the lower things, the second like the square, and the lowest like the Cube, both in the composition of the world's heavens and in the constitution of the creatures within them. 

From this it is clear that universal Harmony occurs by descending, and by no means from the circular motion of the Planets; for these did not exist in the nature of things until after the mundane fabric was completed, which was made step by step by descending, as we gather from the Holy Scriptures. And thus it is established from this that the intentions of Plato and Pythagoras were far different than to adapt the mysteries of the five regular solids to the motions of the Planets; rather, they referred them to the distances of the elements from one another, by which the harmony of form and matter differed because of the variety of proportions. 

But truly, the Arithmetic and Geometry of this kind, by which this Harmony is made, are not common, but truly formal, comprised under this Hebrew word <term>Gematria</term> <note>A Kabbalistic method of interpreting the hidden meaning of words through the numerical values of their letters.</note>; which is concerned with the deep meditation of <term>Kabbalah</term>, <term>Natural Magic</term>, and <term>True Alchemy</term>—that is, the science of the Elements, the celestial, and the supramundane or intelligible realm. We conclude, therefore, that we have first the Holy Scriptures and also the reasons of the most excellent philosophers defending this opinion of ours regarding mundane Harmony, which seem in every way opposed to the foundations of Kepler. 

I likewise have a most exact demonstration of this mundane Harmony in an experiment on a certain most noble creature, in the dissection <note>original: *anatomizatione*</note> of which I observed all these things with my eyes and mind; this I have decided to publish at the end and completion of my works for the common benefit. Although this is a small secret compared to that greatest mystery which the most celebrated Brothers of the <term>Rosy Cross</term> <note>The Rosicrucians, a legendary secret society of mystics and healers that Fludd famously defended.</note> claim to understand; yet, just as they themselves understand and possess the celestial Bread of Angels (as I well know), about which the Holy Scriptures have sung such wonders everywhere, so also I shall expose the physical secrets of the terrestrial bread, perceived by no one until now as far as I know, for the public good, God favoring. I shall call that treatise of mine *The Minor Key of Philosophy*, or *The Ocular Experiment*; because the title *The Key of True Philosophy* most rightly belongs to their own secret, which seems to differ not at all from that Key of David, as may be gathered from their writings. 

Finally, as for the Harmony of the Planets, we say that it is proper to them and ordained by the Creator, so that in those proportions by which they are naturally moved, *they might be signs for fixed times, and for days, and years* <note>Genesis 1:14</note>, and so that in their daily motions—namely, of the Sun—light might be present to the earth for the daytime and rule the day, while the Moon with the other stars might have the power to obtain dominion over the night (Psalm 136:8). We do not read in the Holy Scriptures that they were ordained by God for any other use; although the Sun of heaven, in its acquired perfection, seems to perform wonders. For that Sun is the one and only surpassing Wisdom, but invisible in itself; present everywhere by night and day, exhibiting and liberally pouring out glory and life to every creature. Without this "morning star," there is no true science of Astronomy, no certain prediction of Astrologers, nor any right knowledge of mundane Harmony.

And so I have successfully woven together this discourse on the final part of Johannes Kepler’s Appendix—not as I wished, but as I was able—not invited to this work by my own will, but incited by the stings of another’s envy <note>A reference to Kepler’s published attacks on Fludd's work, which forced Fludd to respond.</note>, with immense thanks to God the Best and Greatest going before me.

->F I N I S.<-

He announces his future work, "The Minor Key of Philosophy," and aligns his mystical theories with the Rosicrucian brotherhood.</summary>
